The hijacker-pilot [Marwan al-Shehhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_al-Shehhi) carried out a suicide dive on the [South Tower](19372810.md) (left) with [United Airlines Flight 175](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_175) during the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md), New York City

[^cite_note-1]





Two [Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze) pilots: Lt. Yoshinori Yamaguchi's [Yokosuka D4Y](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokosuka_D4Y) (left) in a suicide dive against [USS *Essex*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Essex_\(CV-9\)) on 25 November 1944 and pilot [Yukio Araki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukio_Araki) (right) died in a suicide attack on 27 May 1945, [at age 17](#Children_and_teenagers)

A **suicide attack** (also known by a wide variety of other names, [see below](#Definition_and_terminology)) is a deliberate [attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strike_\(attack\)) in which the perpetrators [intentionally end their own lives](16831059.md) as part of the attack. These attacks are a form of [murder–suicide](1468613.md) that is often associated with [terrorism](30636.md) or [war](33158.md). When the attackers are [labelled as terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Definition_of_terrorism), the attacks are sometimes referred to as an act of *suicide terrorism*.[^cite_note-Moghadam_2006-2] Military use of suicide is not directly regulated by [international law](8195726.md), but suicide attacks sometimes violate prohibitions against [perfidy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perfidy) or [targeting civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilian_casualty).[^cite_note-Hunter_2015_(AOAV)-3] Suicide attacks have occurred in various contexts, ranging from military campaigns—such as the Japanese *[kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)* pilots during [World War II](32927.md) (1944–1945)—to more contemporary [Islamic terrorist](566821.md) campaigns—including the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) in 2001. Suicide attacks have been used by a wide range of political ideologies, from [far-right](221220.md) ([Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) and [Germany](21212.md) in WWII) to [far-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-left) (such as the [PKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKK) and [JRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Red_Army)).[^cite_note-IWM_transcript-4][^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5]

## Definition and terminology

See also: [Suicide terminology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_terminology)

### Kamikaze

Further information: [Kamikaze § Definition and origin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze#Definition_and_origin), [Kamikaze (typhoon)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze_\(typhoon\)), [Japanese Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Empire), and [Suicide attack § Japanese Empire](#Japanese_Empire)

[Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze) was a term initially used for Japanese suicide pilots in [World War II](32927.md), but is occasionally used in other contexts.

For example, the 1972 [Lod Airport massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport_massacre) in Israel by the [Japanese Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Red_Army) (JRA) was labelled by some reports at the time a "[Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)" attack, but others have criticized the label, including the surviving attacker's interpreter.[^cite_note-col_rep-6][^cite_note-Satoshi_Sugawara-7] The Kamikaze were a [unit of suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Special_Attack_Units) of the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) in WWII, which had a very different ideology to the JRA. Researchers from [Duke University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_University) described the JRA's motives as "rooted in [anti-imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-imperialism), [anti-colonialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-colonialism), and [anti-capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalism)".[^cite_note-Global_Revolution-8] In more recent reports the 1972 JRA attack on Lod airport is described in modern terms such as "suicide attack" and "suicide mission", even when referring to the attacker who survived.[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5] All three militants intended to die, but one survived.[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5] He confessed and hoped to be quickly executed, but some attribute this to retrospective "[survivor guilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survivor_guilt)".[^cite_note-Okamoto_hope-9]

### Suicide bombing

The term "suicide bombing" dates back to at least 1940 when a *[New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times)* article mentioned the term in relation to German tactics.[^cite_note-10] Less than two years later, the *New York Times* referred to a Japanese *[kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)* attempt on an American carrier as a "suicide bombing".[^cite_note-11] In 1945, *[The Times of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_London)* referred to a *kamikaze* plane as a "suicide-bomb".[^cite_note-12] Two years later, it referred to a new British pilot-less, radio-controlled rocket missile as originally designed "as a counter-measure to the Japanese 'suicide-bomber'."[^cite_note-13]

### Labelling attacks as suicide&#x20;

Further information on [suicide](16831059.md): [ terminology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_terminology), [ legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_legislation), [ religious views](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_views_on_suicide), and [proxy bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxy_bomb)

The definition of "[suicide](16831059.md)" in this context is also a matter of debate.

Suicide terrorism itself has been defined by [Ami Pedahzur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ami_Pedahzur?action=edit\&redlink=1), a professor at the [University of Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Haifa),[^cite_note-Professor_Pedahzur-14] as "violent actions perpetrated by people who are aware that the odds they will return alive are close to zero".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedahzur20048-15] Other sources exclude from their work "suicidal" or high risk attacks, such as the [Lod Airport massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport_massacre) or a "reckless charge in battle".[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16][^cite_note-Dodd-17] Despite the [Lod Airport massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport_massacre) being explicitly planned as a suicide attack, and modern mainstream Israeli and international media describing the event as a "suicide attack" or "[suicide mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission)".[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5] [Yoram Schweitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoram_Schweitzer), from the [International Institute for Counter-Terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Counter-Terrorism) (an Israeli think tank), focuses only on true "suicide attacks", where the odds of survival are not "close to zero" but required to be zero, because "the perpetrator's ensured death is a precondition for the success of his mission".[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16]

The narrower definition would also exclude the actions of groups such as those by the [Hashishiyeen](#Hashishiyeen), by the [Moro juramentado](#Moro_juramentado), and in [Aceh during WWII](#Aceh_in_WWII) ([see below](#Attackers_killed_by_their_opponents)). [Adam Lankford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Lankford?action=edit\&redlink=1) also excludes from the definition are "[proxy bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxy_bomb)", which may have political goals and be designed to look like a suicide bombing. The difference is that the "proxy" is forced to carry a bomb under threat, or the proxy isn't fully aware that they are delivering a bomb that will kill them. The definition also generally excludes [mass shootings](31883778.md) in which the perpetrators commit suicide, as the shooter committing suicide is a separate act from shooting their victims.

Further distinction is how many of such shootings are driven by personal and psychological reasons, rather than political, social or religious motives, such as the [Columbine High School massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbine_High_School_massacre), the [Virginia Tech shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Tech_shooting) or [Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting) in the [United States](50822291.md).[^cite_note-LANKFORD-17-12-2012-18] However, in 1999 the [Columbine High School massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbine_High_School_massacre) was widely labelled as a suicide attack: both attackers shot themselves, but they planned the attack as a bombing.[^cite_note-NYT_25_April_1999-19][^cite_note-1999_updated_2011-20] It is also a common [trope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic_trope) that Muslim attackers are defined as terrorists while others are defined as mentally ill.

It may not always be clear to investigators which type of killing is which, since suicide attack campaigns sometimes use proxy bombers, as alleged in Iraq,[^cite_note-Saudi_fighter_threatened-21] or manipulate the vulnerable to become bombers.[^cite_note-Dodd-17][^cite_note-Azami_Dawood-22] [Adam Lankford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Lankford?action=edit\&redlink=1), also argues that the motivation to kill and be killed connects some suicide attackers more closely to "suicidal rampage" murderers than is commonly thought.[^cite_note-LANKFORD-17-12-2012-18]

### Religious terminology for attackers and victims

Further information: [Abrahamic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions), [Religious views on suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_views_on_suicide), [Islamic view of death § Suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_view_of_death#Suicide), and [Jewish views on suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_views_on_suicide)

All [Abrahamic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions) forbid suicide.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] Suicide and suicide attempts have been decriminalised in most of the western world, but remain criminalised in some countries, such as Afghanistan,[^cite_note-countries_afghanistan-25] Nigeria,[^cite_note-Nigeria_9_October_2021-26] Palestine,[^cite_note-5_October_2023-27] and others.[^cite_note-decrim_countries-28][^cite_note-9_September_2021-29][^cite_note-unitedgmh-30] Terminology relating to this sin or crime is used by Jews, Christians, and Muslims to condemn suicide attackers (see also above), different terminology is usually used to describe self sacrifice that they believe is justified, including actions their enemies label suicide attacks (see below).

#### Martyrdom of attackers and victims&#x20;

Further information on [Martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom) and conflict: [Martyrdom in Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam), [ Shahid (trope)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic_trope#Shahid), [Chahid (Algeria)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chahid_\(Algeria\)), [Military Saint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Saint), [21 Coptic Martyrs of Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21_Coptic_Martyrs_of_Libya), [Martyrs of the Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrs_of_the_Spanish_Civil_War), [Martyrdom in Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Judaism), and [Self-sacrifice in Jewish law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-sacrifice_in_Jewish_law)



Plaque marking the location of the [assassination of Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Benazir_Bhutto), written in [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu). Translation: *Place of Martyrdom, Ms. Benazir Bhutto martyred*. [Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto) was killed by a suicide terrorist in 2007.

[^cite_note-Benazir-31]

Among Muslims, secular Arabs, and related cultures, the term martyr or *shaheed* has a broad meaning and can refer to leaders who have been assassinated or executed, civilian casualties of war, and combatants who did not intend to die.[^cite_note-shahid_in_Israel-32]

[Victims of suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Victims_of_terrorism) and [the bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Suicide_bombings) are both commonly referred to as [martyrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrs), by opposing groups, or when referring to different attacks. Some Arabic speaking militant groups and their supporters call suicide attacks "[martyrdom operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Martyrdom_operations)" ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): للعمليات الاستشهادية, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *aleamaliaat alistishhadia*).[^cite_note-33] The concept of [martyrdom in Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam) is broad including people who died in plagues and women who died in childbirth, as well as fallen combatants who did not intend to die.[^cite_note-34] According to Israeli academic [Assaf Moghadam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assaf_Moghadam), the term "martyrdom operation" has been used by [Hamas](13913.md), [Al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Martyrs'_Brigades), [Fatah](11104.md)'s [Al Aqsa Martyrs Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Aqsa_Martyrs_Brigades), and other Palestinian factions.[^cite_note-Moghadam-16-35] The term "martyrdom operation" was also used in [March 2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2003) by the [Iraqi](7515928.md) administration to refer to suicide attacks on invading troops during the [2003 Iraq war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Iraq_war), and in particular their promise to retake the [Baghdad airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdad_airport).

[Victims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Victims_of_terrorism) of suicide attacks are also referred to as martyrs by a wide variety of cultures. [Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto), the leader of the [Pakistan People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_People's_Party), is the most famous example of a progressive Muslim who is regarded as a martyr after being murdered in a suicide attack. Bhutto was [assassinated in 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Benazir_Bhutto) by a teenage Islamic extremist.[^cite_note-injury-36][^cite_note-exhumation-37] After her death, many things in Pakistan, mostly related to education, were named or renamed in her honour, referring to her by the title *"shaheed"* (martyr).[^cite_note-Benazir-31]

When [an ISIS-inspired suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_attacks#ISIS) blew himself up at [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing) in 2017, he was described by locals and in Arabic package media as a suicide bomber ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): فجر انتحاري or الانتحاري), not a martyr.[^cite_note-Five_sources-38][^cite_note-AJ-39][^cite_note-40] The border guard who was killed attempting to stop him crossing into Egypt, a member of Hamas' Qassam Brigades, was described in official statements and Arabic language media as a martyr ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): الشهيد نضال الجعفري, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *al-Shaheed Nidal al-Jaafari*) and he became a martyr ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): استشهاد, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *[Istishhad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istishhad)*, [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): استشهاد نضال جمعة, [lit.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literal_translation) 'Martyrdom of Nidal al-Jaafari').[^cite_note-anger_over_deviant_ideology-41][^cite_note-shms_Rafah_bomber_family-42][^cite_note-paldf-43], some international media, and the bomber's family. His family condemned him, describing ISIS member's action as completely criminal and anti-Palestine, and announced they would not be holding funeral services for the ISIS suicide-bomber.[^cite_note-alquds_Rafah_martyr-44][^cite_note-shms_Rafah_bomber_family-42] Gaza's clans referred to the bombing as suicide terrorism ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): العمل الإرهابي الانتحاري).[^cite_note-alquds_Rafah_martyr-44] The bomber was also posthumously disowned by family, who announced they would be holding no funeral.[^cite_note-NA_18_August_2017-45][^cite_note-46]

#### Biblical references

See also: [King Saul § Suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Saul#Suicide), [Samson § Death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson#Death), [Judges 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judges_16), [Daniel 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_5), and [Suicide in antiquity § Biblical accounts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_in_antiquity#Biblical_accounts)



&#x20;Illustration of Samson by [Gustave Doré](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9's_illustrations_for_La_Grande_Bible_de_Tours).

[^cite_note-Gustave-50]

In [Israel](9282173.md), [Samson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson)'s words, in [Judges 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judges_16)\:30 ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): תמות נפשי עם פלשתים, [lit.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literal_translation) 'let me die with the Philistines', [Biblical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblical_Hebrew) [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): תָּמוֹת נַפְשִׁי עִם-פְּלִשְׁתִּים).[^cite_note-kikar-51][^cite_note-yNet_Ali-52][^cite_note-Judges_16-53], are quoted as a justification for acts of self sacrifice in battle. The same biblical quote is cited in both praise and criticism of this approach to warfare.[^cite_note-kikar-51][^cite_note-rev_globes-54] Prior to the establishment of the State of Israel, this story was cited by two of the [anti-British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Mandatory_Palestine) pre-state militant groups in reference to their [premeditated plans](#1947).[^cite_note-Sheleg_2007_suicide_operation-55] One leader claimed that two militants who blew themselves up had not committed suicide, as such, due to [allegedly mitigating circumstances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olei_Hagardom).[^cite_note-murdered_by_the_British-56][^cite_note-Sheleg_2007_approval-57] Their modern critics claim the situation itself was largely self-inflicted.[^cite_note-study_unit-58]

Some within Israel view the Samson in a very negative light.[^cite_note-Rinon_2024-59] People from Christian backgrounds, or people who live within majority-Christian communities, have carried out suicide attacks [in Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacy_Hryniewiecki), [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sana'a_Mehaidli), [the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath_School_disaster), [New Zealand](#1905_in_New_Zealand), and elsewhere (see below). They have usually not used religious language to explain or justify their actions. Most have been members or supporters of secular movements, or they have perpetrated [isolated attacks](#personal) and they did not give lengthy reasons as to why they perpetrated the attacks.[^cite_note-lewis-2013-60][^cite_note-Nassau-61][^cite_note-1905_in_New_Zealand-62] However, in 1959 in [Houston](13774.md), Texas, Paul Harold Orgeron reportedly mentioned "the will of God" before detonating a suitcase bomb that killed himself and 5 other people at [Poe Elementary School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poe_Elementary_School_bombing).[^cite_note-Houstonia-63]

#### Non-Abrahamic religions

[Kuyili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuyili) – who killed herself in a suicide attack in 1780, [see below](#1780) – is revered as *Theepaanchi Amman*, meaning "the goddess who jumped into fire", in Muthupatti village near Sivagangai, Tamil Nadu, based on local oral traditions.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65]

#### Opferanschlag (sacrifice bombing)

In German-speaking areas the term "[sacrifice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacrifice) bombing" ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language): *Opferanschlag*) was proposed in 2012 by German scholar [Arata Takeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arata_Takeda?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-Takeda_sacrifice-66] This is different from the German word used by Nazi Germany to refer to self sacrifice attacks.

Suicide attacks include both "suicide terrorism" and attacks targeting combatants. "[Terrorism](30636.md)" is often defined as any action "intended to cause death or serious bodily harm to civilians or non-combatants" for the purpose of intimidation.[^cite_note-67] This definition is often not used consistently; even those claiming to define terrorism this way sometimes describe attacks on their own military as "terrorism", while attacks on civilians by allied state actors are almost never called terrorism.[^cite_note-disap-68]

An alternative definition provided by [Jason Burke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Burke), a journalist who has lived among Islamic militants, suggests that most define terrorism as "the use or threat of serious violence" to advance some kind of "cause", stressing that terrorism is a tactic.[^cite_note-69] This definition is often referred to by the euphemism "[political violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence)".[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71]

Academic [Fred Halliday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Halliday) has written that assigning the descriptor of "terrorist" or "terrorism" to the actions of a group is a tactic used by states to deny "legitimacy" and "rights to protest and rebel".[^cite_note-72] Israeli diplomacy has been very influential in defining terrorism as a concept.[^cite_note-disap-68][^cite_note-word_terrorism-73][^cite_note-Salon_terror-74] This was largely led by [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin), who himself has been labelled as a terrorist leader, as commander of the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) militant group before Israel was recognized as a nation state by [Western powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_power).[^cite_note-word_terrorism-73][^cite_note-Salon_terror-74]

### Homicide bombing&#x20;

See also: [murder–suicide](1468613.md); [mass murder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_murder); [ mass murders (religious, political, or ethnic)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rampage_killers_\(religious,_political,_or_ethnic_crimes\)); and [Demonizing the enemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demonizing_the_enemy)

Some efforts have been made to replace the term "suicide bombing" with "homicide bombing", based on the assertion that "[homicide](123451.md)" is a more apt adjective than "suicide" since the primary purpose of such a bombing is to kill other people. The only major media outlets to use it were the [Fox News Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News_Channel) and the *[New York Post](102227.md)*, both of which are owned by [News Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_Corporation_\(1980%E2%80%932013\)) and have since mostly abandoned the term.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] Robert Goldney, a professor emeritus at the [University of Adelaide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Adelaide), has argued in favor of the term "homicide bomber". Goldney argued that studies show that there is little in common between people who blow themselves up intending to kill as many people as possible in the process and actual suicide victims.[^cite_note-77] Fox News producer Dennis Murray argued that a suicidal act should be reserved for a person who does something to kill only themselves. [CNN](62028.md) producer Christa Robinson argued that the term "homicide bomber" was not specific enough, stating that "A homicide bomber could refer to someone planting a bomb in a trash can".[^cite_note-Moghadam-16-35][^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]

## Background

This article is about intentional use of suicide. For unsuccessfully avoided deaths, see [suicide in the military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_in_the_military).

Initially, these attacks primarily targeted military, police, and public officials. This approach continued with groups like [Al-Qaeda](1921.md), which combined mass civilian targets with political leadership.[^cite_note-Moghadam_2006-2] While only a few suicide attacks occurred between 1945 and 1980,[^cite_note-Pape-2003-80] between 1981 and September 2015 a total of 4,814 suicide attacks were carried out in over 40 countries,[^cite_note-CPoST-81] resulting in over 45,000 deaths. The global frequency of these attacks increased from an average of three per year in the 1980s to roughly one per month in the 1990s, almost one per week from 2001 to 2003,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006128-82] and roughly one per day from 2003 to 2015.[^cite_note-CPoST-81] In 2019, there were 149 suicide bombings in 24 countries, carried out by 236 individuals. These attacks resulted in 1,850 deaths and 3,660 injuries.[^cite_note-Rinehart_2021-06-15-83]

### Related actions

Main article: [suicide mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission)

#### Suicide protests

Main article: [Suicide protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_protest)

Further information: [Self-immolation](916669.md), [Hunger strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger_strikes), [Hunger strikes in Turkey (2016–2017)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger_strikes_in_Turkey_\(2016%E2%80%932017\)), [Suffragette bombing and arson campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffragette_bombing_and_arson_campaign), and [Hunger Strike Medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger_Strike_Medal)

Suicide is sometimes used as an extreme act of protest, without intent to cause tangible damage to the opponent.[^cite_note-84] [Romas Kalanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romas_Kalanta) was a 19-year-old Lithuanian student who self-immolated in 1972 to protest against the [Soviet regime in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic), sparking the [1972 unrest in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_unrest_in_Lithuania); another 13 people self-immolated in that same year.[^cite_note-85] [Hunger strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger_strikes) are another use of self harm, and actual or potential suicide, that is used by some militant groups.

#### Self-sacrifice to prevent other casualties

See also: [Falling on a grenade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falling_on_a_grenade), [Altruistic suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altruistic_suicide), and [Suicide mission § Self-sacrifice to prevent other casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Self-sacrifice_to_prevent_other_casualties)

|  | This section **may be [too long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Section#Section_size_policies) or excessively detailed.** It needs a sentence, not a section. *(September 2025)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |

During the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), [IDF](39237.md) Major [Roi Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roi_Klein) and his unit took part in the [Battle of Bint Jbeil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bint_Jbeil_\(2006\)). During a [Hezbollah](13919.md) ambush, a [hand grenade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_grenade) was thrown over the wall that was between Hezbollah militants and Klein and his unit. Klein jumped on the live grenade and muffled the explosion with his body. The soldiers reported that Klein recited the Jewish prayer, [Shema Yisrael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shema_Yisrael), as he jumped on the grenade. He then handed over his encoded radio to another officer, who took command of the force, and died.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-Lubotzky-87][^cite_note-JP071709-88][^cite_note-Meotti-89][^cite_note-Katz-90] According to *The Telegraph* he yelled "Long live Israel",[^cite_note-TDT080907-91] although this was probably a misinterpretation of "Shema Yisrael" (שמע ישראל).[^cite_note-92]

#### Preventing capture

Further information: [Suicide mission § Preventing capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Preventing_capture), [Suicide pill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_pill), [Hannibal Directive § Controversy within the IDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Directive#Controversy_within_the_IDF), [First Jewish–Roman War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War), [Siege of Masada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Masada), [Masada myth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada_myth), [Mass suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_suicide), and [Suicide Cliff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_Cliff)

During the [First Jewish–Roman War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War), the letter of the [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) determined that the people in the [besieged city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Masada) of [Masada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada) should commit [mass suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_suicide), and even murder family members, to avoid capture during the [Siege of Masada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Masada).[^cite_note-Rinon_2024-59] The [story of Masada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada_myth) is prominent in Israeli culture.[^cite_note-Rinon_2024-59] The [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) Jewish sect are also sometimes described as carrying out "suicidal" attacks against their enemies ([see below](#Sicarii)).[^cite_note-Masada-93]

Other than as a way to cause enemy casualties, another situation in which some militaries and related bodies (such as intelligence agencies) encourage their own members to commit suicide is to avoid being captured by the enemy. The concept also often includes the use of intentional [friendly fire](202484.md).[^cite_note-rev_globes-54] Either to avoid disclose of military secrets, [avoid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Directive) the [need for a prisoner exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilad_Shalit_prisoner_exchange), or for more intangible ideological motives.[^cite_note-Masada-93] Individuals are encouraged by a perception that capture is a fate worse than death, and the likelihood of [torture](47702.md) is strongly emphasised in internal propaganda. Sometimes, to the point that even civilians embrace the concept of dying (or killing people on their own side) to avoid capture.[^cite_note-unimaginable-94][^cite_note-JP_844045-95]

In some cases, [special forces of Western militaries have](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission) been provided with [suicide pills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_pills) to take if they are at risk of capture.[^cite_note-Freya_CBC-96][^cite_note-JN_JC-97] Some militaries of nation states avoid equipping their troops with any means specifically designed to facilitate suicide, but imply that soldiers are obliged to resort to extreme measures to avoid capture including taking their own lives, or killing their comrades, with whatever means are available.[^cite_note-rev_globes-54][^cite_note-Brody_2015-98][^cite_note-Brody_2024-99] Hand grenades have been repeatedly used or recommended.[^cite_note-tr_49008320-100][^cite_note-rev_globes-54] In 1952, three Chinese soldiers reportedly killed themselves with hand grenades to avoid capture.[^cite_note-tr_49008320-100] Military commanders have reportedly recommended explosives such as hand grenades as a means of suicide, for the advantage of killing as many of the would-be captors as possible, thus converting the situation into an improvised suicide attack.[^cite_note-rev_globes-54]

### Attackers killed by their opponents

Main article: [Suicide mission § Attacks against stronger opponents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Attacks_against_stronger_opponents)

See also: [Martyrdom in Islam § Willingness to die in Battle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Willingness_to_die_in_Battle), and [Suicide attack § Labelling as suicide](#Labelling_as_suicide)

Some sources define "suicide terrorism" and "suicide attack" broadly to include attackers who are killed by other people.[^cite_note-101] At the opposite extreme, militant leaders of from [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_terrorism) or [Islamic militant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_militant) groups – [both religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions) that [forbid suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_views_on_suicide) – claim that directly causing one's own death sometimes is not suicide (see above: [suicide](#suicide) and [religious terminology](#Religious_terminology)). However, followers of these religiously derived ideologies define their enemies as suicidal, even if their opponent uses the same technique, such as when an [ISIS](#ISIS)-linked [suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_attacks#ISIS) killed [al-Qassam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qassam) border guard [Nidal al-Jaafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nidal_al-Jaafari?action=edit\&redlink=1) at [Rafah crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_crossing) ([see above](#martyrdom)). During the colonial era and up to [World War II](32927.md), Muslims in [Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh) and [Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_people), now part of Indonesia and the Philippines, attacked much more powerful opponents – principally the Dutch, the Japanese, and the Americans – despite near certain death. Some authors have characterised these as predecessors of modern suicide bombings.[^cite_note-Indigenous_roots-102] Other sources say that suicide bombings in places like the Philippines arrose only recently, due to foreign influence from international cultists such as ISIS.[^cite_note-rsis_2020_Jolo-103][^cite_note-Jolo_24_Aug_2020-104][^cite_note-Estabillo_3_July_2019-105]

#### Sicarii (first century AD)

Further information: [§ First](#First), [§ Preventing capture](#Preventing_capture), [Suicide mission § Preventing capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Preventing_capture), [Jewish terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_terrorism), [First Jewish–Roman War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War), [Masada myth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada_myth), and [Sicari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicari)

The [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) Jewish sect are sometimes described as carrying out "suicidal" attacks against their enemies. Riaz Hassan said that the first-century AD Jewish [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) sect carried out "suicidal missions to kill" [Hellenized Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenized_Jews) they considered immoral collaborators.[^cite_note-Masada-93] An article in [BBC Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Arabic) originally called the [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) the first group to carry out suicide attacks. In September 2023, over a year after initial publication, this attracted controversy in English language Israeli media, when a complaint led to a change in the article.[^cite_note-JP_759136-106][^cite_note-i24news_20230917-107] [The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post) classified the article under "antisemtitism".[^cite_note-JP_759136-106] The [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) sect play a central role in [Israeli mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada_myth).[^cite_note-Rinon_2024-59] The Sicarii killed themselves and their families [to avoid capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Preventing_capture) ([see above](#Sicarii)), but in their attacks they were [killed by their opponents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Attackers_killed_by_their_opponents).[^cite_note-Rinon_2024-59] The Sicarii carried out attacks knowing it would lead to their own executions.[^cite_note-JP_759136-106] The revised BBC article claimed the first suicide attacks were by the [Order of Assassins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Assassins), who are also [killed by their opponents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Order_of_Assassins) (see next section).[^cite_note-JP_759136-106][^cite_note-i24news_20230917-107]

#### Order of Assassins (1090 to 1275)&#x20;

Main article: [Order of Assassins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Assassins)

The [Order of Assassins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Assassins) ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): حَشّاشِین, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *Hashishiyeen*; [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): حشاشين, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Ḥaššāšīn*) were from a sect of [Ismaili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismaili) [Shi'a Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Muslims). They assassinated two [Caliphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caliphs), as well as many viziers, Sultans, and [Crusade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades) leaders over 300 years,[^cite_note-108] before being annihilated by [Mongol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongols) invaders. *Hashishiyeen* were known for targeting the powerful, using the dagger as a weapon (rather than something safer for the assassin such as a crossbow), and for not attempting to escape after completing their killing.[^cite_note-ARSI-BL-xii-109]

#### Moro juramentado&#x20;

Further information: [Juramentado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juramentado), [Martyrdom in Islam § Juramentado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Juramentado), [Suicide mission § Juramentado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Juramentado), and [Terrorism in the Philippines](9101671.md)

[Juramentado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juramentado), in [Philippine](23440.md) [history](23441.md), refers to a male [Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_people) [swordsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swordsman#Filipino) (from the [Tausug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tausug_people) tribe of [Sulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Islands)) who attacked and killed targeted occupying and invading [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police) and [soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier). Death was expected, and considered [martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom), undertaken as a form of [jihad](16203.md).[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114]

[Moro people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_people) who performed suicide attacks were called *mag-sabil*, and the suicide attacks were known as *parang-sabil*.[^cite_note-115] The Spanish called them *[juramentados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juramentado)*. The idea of the *juramentado* was considered part of *[jihad](16203.md)* in the Moros' Islamic religion. During an attack, a *juramentado* would throw himself at his targets and kill them with bladed weapons such as [barongs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barong_\(knife\)) and [kris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kris) until he was killed. The Moros performed *juramentado* suicide attacks against the Spanish in the [Spanish–Moro conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93Moro_conflict) of the 16th to the 19th centuries, against the Americans in the [Moro Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moro_Rebellion) from 1899 to 1913), and [against the Japanese in World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_resistance_against_Japan#Moro_resistance_in_Mindanao).[^cite_note-116]

The Moro ([Spanish](26825.md): *juramentados*) launched suicide attacks on the Japanese, Spanish, Americans and Filipinos, but did not attack the non-Muslim Chinese as the Chinese were not considered enemies of the Moro people.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121] The Japanese responded to these suicide attacks by massacring all known family members and relatives of the attackers.[^cite_note-122]

According to historian [Stephan Dale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephan_Dale?action=edit\&redlink=1), the Moro were not the only culture who carried out suicide attacks "in their fight against Western hegemony and colonial rule". In the 18th century, suicide tactics were used on the [Malabar Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malabar_Coast) of southwestern India, and in [Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh) in Northern [Sumatra](28679.md) as well (see above).[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16][^cite_note-123]

#### Aceh War (1873–1904)&#x20;

See also: [Suicide mission § Aceh War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Aceh_War), [Martyrdom in Islam § Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Aceh), [Suicide attack § Dutch](#Dutch), and [§ Aceh in WWII](#Aceh_in_WWII)



The former [Dutch East Indies](23476997.md) (dark red) within the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) (light red) at its furthest extent



Location of [Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh) on a map of present day [Indonesia](14579.md).

Muslim [Acehnese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acehnese_people) from the [Aceh Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh_Sultanate) performed suicide attacks known as *parang-sabil* against Dutch invaders during the [Aceh War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh_War) (1873–1904). It was considered part of personal *[jihad](16203.md)* in Islam. The Dutch called it *Atjèh-moord*, (lit. Aceh murder).[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] The Acehnese work of literature the *[Hikayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hikayat) Perang Sabil* provided the background and reasoning for the *Atjèh-moord* as Acehnese suicide attacks upon the Dutch.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] The Indonesian translations of the Dutch terms are *Aceh bodoh*, *Aceh pungo*, *Aceh gila*, or *Aceh mord*.[^cite_note-130]

#### Aceh in WWII

See also: [Suicide mission § Aceh in WWII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Aceh_in_WWII), [Suicide attack § Aceh War](#Aceh_War), [§ Dutch](#Dutch), and [§ During World War II](#During_World_War_II)

*[Atjèh-moord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atj%C3%A8h-moord?action=edit\&redlink=1)* was also used against the Japanese by the [Acehnese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acehnese_people) during the [Japanese occupation of Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_the_Dutch_East_Indies#The_occupation).[^cite_note-131] The [Acehnese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acehnese_people) *[Ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulama)* (Islamic Scholars) fought against both the Dutch and the Japanese, revolting against the Dutch in February 1942 and against Japan in November 1942. The revolt was led by the All-Aceh Religious Scholars' Association (PUSA). The Japanese suffered 18 dead in the uprising while they slaughtered either up to 100 or over 120 Acehnese.[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133] The revolt happened in Bayu and was centred around Tjot Plieng village's religious school.[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137] During the revolt, the Japanese troops armed with mortars and machine guns were charged by sword wielding Acehnese under *Teungku Abduldjalil* (Tengku Abdul Djalil) in [Buloh Gampong Teungah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buloh_Gampong_Teungah?action=edit\&redlink=1) on 10 November and [Tjot Plieng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tjot_Plieng?action=edit\&redlink=1) on 13 November.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] In May 1945 the Acehnese rebelled again.[^cite_note-140]

#### Disposable use of troops during WWI and WWII&#x20;

Further information: [Human wave attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_wave_attack), [Forlorn hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forlorn_hope), [Gallipoli landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallipoli_landing), [Anzac Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anzac_Day), and [Suicide attack § During World War Two](#During_World_War_Two)

Supreme Allied Commander [Lord Mountbatten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Mountbatten) claimed high casualties were justified during the [Dieppe Raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dieppe_Raid), "For every man that died at [Dieppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dieppe), ten were saved on [D-Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-Day)".[^cite_note-JN_JC-97][^cite_note-141] From one frito of twelve (11 Canadians and one British), only two returned alive, the others were killed or captured.[^cite_note-Freya_CBC-96] One of the survivors was a [British radar expert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Nissenthall), who had been given a [cyanide pill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyanide_pill) to take if he was captured.[^cite_note-Freya_CBC-96]

#### Suicide by cop

A woman attempting suicide by cop in [Flagler County, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flagler_County,_Florida)

Suicide by cop, also known as suicide by police or law-enforcement-assisted suicide,[^cite_note-142] is a term used to define a death, intended as [suicide](16831059.md), for which a person attempts to provoke [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police). The phenomenon, as described in some sources, involves an allegedly [suicidal person](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_crisis) deliberately behaving in a threatening manner with intent to provoke law enforcement to kill them.[^cite_note-Stincelli-143] The concept arose in the United States.[^cite_note-144] The terminology has now spread to other places such as Canada, Australia, and non-English-speaking countries such as Austria.[^cite_note-The_Age_22_March_2011-145][^cite_note-146]

## First suicide attacks (attackers who killed themselves)&#x20;

See also: [History of suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_suicide), [World War I](4764461.md), and [Interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period)

Several different events have been described as the first suicide attack in different sources.[^cite_note-thebetterindia-147][^cite_note-queen-velu-148]

The oldest story that is widely described as a suicide attack in modern sources is the biblical story of [Samson's destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson#Suicide_attack) of the [Philistine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philistine) temple in [Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Gaza) in [Judges 16\:30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judges_16\:30).[^cite_note-alarabiya_sam-149][^cite_note-first_shahid-150][^cite_note-Takeda_2010-151][^cite_note-mythic_discourse-152] A study by German scholar [Arata Takeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arata_Takeda?action=edit\&redlink=1) analyzes analogous behavior represented in literary texts from the antiquity through the 20th century, these being *[Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajax_\(Sophocles\))*, *[Samson Agonistes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson_Agonistes)*, *[The Robbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Robbers)*, and *[The Just Assassins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Just_Assassins)*. The study concluded "that suicide bombings are not the expressions of specific cultural peculiarities or exclusively religious fanaticisms. Instead, they represent a strategic option of the desperately weak who strategically disguise themselves under the mask of apparent strength, terror, and invincibility".[^cite_note-mythic_discourse-152][^cite_note-Takeda_2010-151] Other writers have also interpreted Samson's death [as a suicide attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson#Suicide_attack).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrury2003-153][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWicker2003-154][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtiya1973-155]

An article in [BBC Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Arabic) claimed the [Sicarii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicarii) were the first group to carry out suicide attacks. In September 2023 the BBC responded to a complaint, deleted the Sicarii and reworded the second example so that the revised article called the [Order of Assassins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Assassins) ([Ismaeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismaeli_Shia) [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Shia_Muslims)) the first.[^cite_note-JP_759136-106][^cite_note-i24news_20230917-107] However, both groups were [killed by their opponents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Attackers_killed_by_their_opponents) ([see above](#Attackers_killed_by_their_opponents)).

### Arnold von Winkelried (1386 in Switzerland)&#x20;

[Arnold von Winkelried](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_von_Winkelried) was considered a hero in the Swiss struggle for independence for sacrificing himself at the [Battle of Sempach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sempach) in 1386.

### Kuyili (1780 in India)&#x20;

Main articles: [Kuyili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuyili) and [Velu Nachiyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velu_Nachiyar)

In 1780, an [Indian](7564733.md) [woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bombers) named [Kuyili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuyili) applied [ghee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghee) and oil onto her body and set herself ablaze. She then jumped into an armoury of the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company), causing it to explode. This suicide attack helped to secure victory for her queen, [Velu Nachiyar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velu_Nachiyar), in the battle.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-thebetterindia-147][^cite_note-queen-velu-148][^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] Historical evidence about her life is scarce, relying primarily on folk songs and oral histories due to her marginalized Arunthathiyar or Sambavar background.[^cite_note-159]

### 17th- and 19th-century Dutch&#x20;

See also: [Suicide attack § Aceh War](#Aceh_War)

In the late 17th century, [Qing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) official [Yu Yonghe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yu_Yonghe) recorded that injured [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company) soldiers fighting against [Koxinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koxinga)'s forces for control of Taiwan in 1661 would use gunpowder to blow up both themselves and their opponents rather than be taken prisoner.[^cite_note-160] However, Yu may have confused such suicidal tactics with the standard Dutch military practice of undermining and blowing up overrun positions, which almost cost Koxinga his life during the [Siege of Fort Zeelandia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Fort_Zeelandia).[^cite_note-161]

On 5 February 1831, during the [Belgian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Revolution), a gale blew a Dutch [gunboat](274471.md) under the command of [Jan van Speyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_van_Speyk) into the quay of the [port of Antwerp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Antwerp). As the ship was stormed by Belgians, van Speyk refused to surrender, instead igniting the ship's gunpowder with either his gun or cigar, blowing up the ship. The explosion killed 28 out of the 31 crewmen and an unknown number of Belgians.[^cite_note-162]

### Ignaty Grinevitsky (1881) and others in Russia&#x20;

Further information: [Ignacy Hryniewiecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacy_Hryniewiecki), [Narodnaya Volya (the People's Will)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narodnaya_Volya), and [Assassination of Alexander II of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Alexander_II_of_Russia)

A Russian man named [Ignaty Grinevitsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignaty_Grinevitsky) (also spelled: [Ignacy Hryniewiecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacy_Hryniewiecki)) is sometimes described as the first known suicide bomber.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis2013-163][^cite_note-AOAV_1881-164] The invention of dynamite in the 1860s presented revolutionary and terrorist groups in Europe with a weapon nearly 20 times more powerful than gunpowder. However, using dynamite required overcoming the technical challenges of detonating it at the right time. One solution was to use a human trigger, which was the technique used to assassinate Tsar [Alexander II of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_II_of_Russia) in 1881.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis2013-163][^cite_note-165] A would-be suicide bomber killed Russian Minister of the Interior [Vyacheslav von Plehve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyacheslav_von_Plehve), in St Petersburg in 1904, but survived with major injuries.[^cite_note-166]

### Personal disputes before WWII&#x20;

See also: [Pilot suicides (below)](#Pilot_suicides), [§ Car bomb suicides](#Car_bomb_suicides), and [1976 Mason County jail bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Mason_County_jail_bombing)

#### 1905 in New Zealand&#x20;

See also: [Murchison, New Zealand § History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murchison,_New_Zealand#History); [Terrorism in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_New_Zealand); [§ NZ 1982](#NZ_1982); and [§ USA 1953](#USA_1953)



&#x20;Commemorative plaque for the [1905 Murchison suicide attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murchison,_New_Zealand#History)&#x20;

The earliest known non-military suicide attack occurred in [Murchison, New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murchison,_New_Zealand), on 14 July 1905. When a long-standing dispute between two farmers resulted in a court case, defendant Joseph Sewell arrived with sticks of [gelignite](226403.md) strapped to his body. During the court proceedings, Sewell shouted "I'll blow the devil to hell, and I have enough dynamite to do just that." He was then ushered out of the building and when a police officer tried to arrest him on the street, Sewell detonated the charge, killing himself. No one other than Sewell was killed by the attack.[^cite_note-1905_in_New_Zealand-62][^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171][^cite_note-Te_Ara_1-172]

#### 1927 Bath School bombings in Michigan, USA&#x20;

Further information: [Bath School disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath_School_disaster), [Andrew Kehoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Kehoe), [Car bomb § History](155282.md#History), [mass murder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_murder), and [Poe Elementary School bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poe_Elementary_School_bombing)

The first reported car bombing was the [Bath School bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath_School_disaster) in [Michigan](18859.md), USA in 1927. Multiple separate explosions on the same day killed 45 people, including the bomber, and half of a school was destroyed. The bombings were all carried out by [Andrew Kehoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Kehoe), motivated by a [personal grievance](#personal). His death was possibly an intentional [suicide](16831059.md), but the cause of the explosion was a gun shot that might not have been intended to set off the load. The explosion itself did not seem to form part of a suicide attack on a specific planned target other than possibly himself and his truck. The explosives in his truck detonated when he saw two men nearby had a gun, after he set off multiple other bombs.[^cite_note-Inquest_Page_326-173][^cite_note-Inquest_Page_352-174] The explosion may have been set off indirectly by him firing his own gun at the men.[^cite_note-Inquest_Winchester-175][^cite_note-Jones_PhD_Dissertation_Page_1-176] Most of the deaths were caused by the earlier bombs.[^cite_note-ellsworth_ch_2-177][^cite_note-Burcar-178]

### Pahartali European Club attack (1932, in Bangladesh)



The Pahartali European Club (shown here in 2010) was torched by the group of revolutionaries.

In 1932, Surya Sen planned an attack on the Pahartali European Club which had a signboard that read "Dogs and Indians not allowed".[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] Sen decided to appoint a woman leader for the mission. As Kalpana Datta was arrested prior to the planned attack, Pritilata was assigned the leadership of the group. Pritilata went to Kotowali sea side for arms training and the plan of their attack was finalised there.[^cite_note-The_Fire-Brand_Woman_Of_Indian_Freedom_Struggle.-182] It was decided to attack the club on 24 September 1932, and the members of the group were given [potassium cyanide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potassium_cyanide), to be swallowed to commit suicide if they were caught. Pritilata herself committed suicide on this attack after sustaining bullet injury.[^cite_note-Banglapedia-183][^cite_note-Kalpana_Dutt_1979_53-184] According to the police report, one woman was killed, and four men and seven women were injured in the attack.[^cite_note-Sonali_Swapno-185]

### Spanish civil war (1936–1939)

See also: [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War) and [Martyrs of the Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrs_of_the_Spanish_Civil_War)

There are a few reports of suicide bombers during the civil war in the 1930s.[^cite_note-first_shahid-150]

### Chinese suicide squads

See also: [Suicide attack § During World War II](#During_World_War_II), [§ Japanese](#Japanese), [§ Korean War](#Korean_War), [Martyrdom in Chinese culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Chinese_culture), and [National Revolutionary Army § Dare to Die Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Revolutionary_Army#Dare_to_Die_Corps)



Chinese suicide bomber putting on a 24 hand grenade-explosive vest prior to attack on Japanese tanks at the [Battle of Taierzhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Taierzhuang).

During the [Xinhai Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinhai_Revolution) and the [Warlord Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warlord_Era) of the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)), "[Dare to Die Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Revolutionary_Army#Dare_to_Die_Corps)" ([traditional Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Chinese_characters): 敢死隊; [simplified Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 敢死队; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *gǎnsǐduì*; [Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade%E2%80%93Giles): *Kan-ssu-tui*) or "suicide squads".[^cite_note-China_multi_ref-186] were frequently used by Chinese armies. China deployed these suicide units against the Japanese during the [Second Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Sino-Japanese_War).

In 1938 300 Chinese troops reportedly killed themselves with hand grenades to avoid being captured.[^cite_note-tr_229868436-187] Hand grenade suicide to avoid capture has also been used by other militaries ([see above](#Preventing_capture)), and allegedly was recommended by the leader of the [Golani Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golani_Brigade) of the [IDF](39237.md).[^cite_note-rev_globes-54]

In the [Xinhai Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinhai_Revolution), many [Chinese revolutionaries became martyrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Chinese_culture) in battle. "Dare to Die" student corps were founded for student revolutionaries wanting to fight against [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) rule. [Sun Yat-sen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Yat-sen) and [Huang Xing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huang_Xing) promoted the corp, Huang saying, "We must die, so let us die bravely."[^cite_note-188] Suicide squads were formed by Chinese students going into battle, knowing that they would be killed fighting against overwhelming odds.[^cite_note-189]

The [72 Martyrs of Huanghuagang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/72_Martyrs_of_Huanghuagang) died in the uprising that began the [Wuchang Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuchang_Uprising). They were recognized as heroes and martyrs by the [Kuomintang](16903.md) party and the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)).[^cite_note-190] The martyrs in the "Dare to Die" corps wrote letters to family members before heading off to certain death. The [Huanghuakang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Guangzhou_Uprising#Memorials) was built as a monument to the 72 dead.[^cite_note-191] The deaths of the revolutionaries helped the establishment of the Republic of China, overthrowing the Qing dynasty.[^cite_note-192] Other "Dare to Die" student corps in the Xinhai revolution were led by students who later became major military leaders in Republic of China, like [Chiang Kai-shek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiang_Kai-shek)[^cite_note-193] and [Huang Shaoxiong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huang_Shaoxiong) with the Muslim [Bai Chongxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bai_Chongxi) against Qing dynasty forces.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] Dare to Die troops were used by warlords.[^cite_note-196] The Kuomintang used one to put down an insurrection in Canton.[^cite_note-197] Many women joined them in addition to men to achieve martyrdom against China's opponents.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199] They were known as 烈士 (*lit-she*; martyrs) after accomplishing their mission.[^cite_note-200]

During the [January 28 Incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_28_Incident) or Shanghai Incident (28 January – 3 March 1932), a Dare to Die squad from the [19th Route Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_Route_Army) attacked Japanese positions during the escalating rioting and violence in the city.[^cite_note-201]

Suicide bombing was also used against the Japanese. The Dare to Die Corps was effectively used against Japanese units at the [Battle of Taierzhuang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Taierzhuang). They used swords[^cite_note-Fenby_2003-202][^cite_note-Fenby_2008-203] and wore suicide vests made out of grenades.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

One Chinese soldier detonated a grenade vest, killing 20 Japanese soldiers in the [defense of Sihang Warehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_of_Sihang_Warehouse). Chinese troops [strapped explosives such as grenade packs or dynamite to their bodies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_belt) and threw themselves under Japanese tanks to destroy them.[^cite_note-206] This tactic was used during the [Battle of Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Shanghai) to stop a Japanese tank column when an attacker exploded himself beneath the lead tank,[^cite_note-207] and at the Battle of Taierzhuang where Chinese troops with dynamite and grenades strapped to themselves rushed the Japanese tanks[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209][^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] in one incident obliterating four Japanese tanks with grenade bundles.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213]

During the 1946–1950 [Communist Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Communist_Revolution), [coolies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coolie), poor laborers, fighting the Communists formed Dare to Die Corps.[^cite_note-214] During the [1989 Tiananmen Square protests and massacre](44534.md), protesting students also formed "Dare to Die Corps" risking their lives defending the leaders of the protest.[^cite_note-215]

## During World War II

### Germans during World War II

See also: [Leonidas Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonidas_Squadron), [Sonderkommando Elbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonderkommando_Elbe), [Mass suicides in Nazi Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_suicides_in_Nazi_Germany), [German resistance to Nazism § Suicide bombing attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_resistance_to_Nazism#Suicide_bombing_attempts), and [Assassination attempts on Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_attempts_on_Adolf_Hitler)



[Rudolf Christoph Freiherr von Gersdorff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolf_Christoph_Freiherr_von_Gersdorff)

[Rudolf Christoph Freiherr von Gersdorff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudolf_Christoph_Freiherr_von_Gersdorff) intended to assassinate [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md) with a suicide bombing in 1943, but was unable to complete the attack.[^cite_note-216]

According to the commander of the [12th SS Panzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_SS_Panzer_Division_Hitlerjugend) Division [Kurt Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_Meyer), several of his men had tied explosives to themselves and jumped on Allied tanks during the [Normandy Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Campaign).[^cite_note-217]

During the [Battle of Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Berlin) the Luftwaffe flew "self-sacrifice missions" ([German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language): *selbstopfereinsätze*) against Soviet bridges over the [River Oder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Oder). These "total missions" were flown by pilots of the [Leonidas Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonidas_Squadron). From 17 to 20 April 1945, using any available aircraft, the Luftwaffe claimed the squadron had destroyed 17 bridges. However, military historian [Antony Beevor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Beevor) believes this claim was exaggerated and only the railway bridge at [Küstrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%BCstrin) was definitely destroyed. He comments that "thirty-five pilots and aircraft was a high price to pay for such a limited and temporary success". The missions were called off when the Soviet ground forces reached the vicinity of the squadron's airbase at [Jüterbog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%BCterbog).[^cite_note-218]

The Luftwaffe also formed the [Sonderkommando Elbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonderkommando_Elbe) squadron late in the war, flying [Bf 109](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bf_109) in ramming attacks on Allied bombers. In their lone mission on 7 April 1945, a force of 180 Bf 109s managed to ram 15 Allied bombers, downing 8 of them.[^cite_note-219]Though widely described as suicide missions, the pilots were expected to [parachute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parachute) out just before or after the ramming attack.[^cite_note-220]

### Destruction of Auschwitz crematorium IV (1944)

Main article: [Auschwitz Sonderkommando revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auschwitz_Sonderkommando_revolt)

The [Sonderkommando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonderkommando) [revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolt) at [Auschwitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auschwitz) on 7 October 1944 is widely described as a [suicide mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission).

Most (452) of the rebel prisoners were [killed by](#Attackers_killed_by_their_opponents) the [SS guards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SS_guards) during or soon after the uprising. Rebels armed with work tools and [shivs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shivs) attacked SS guards who were armed with pistols and machine guns.[^cite_note-Sonderkommando_nationalww2museum-221]

Some of the Sonderkommando prisoners took the already suicidal mission a step further. They set fire to Crematorium IV while they were inside the building, and stayed inside to prevent the guards putting out the fire until the building was destroyed. The fire possibly also ignited explosives they had placed in the walls.[^cite_note-Sonderkommando-222][^cite_note-Sonderkommando_nationalww2museum-221]

The [Polish resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_resistance_movement_in_World_War_II) outside the camp tried to postpone the uprising until the [Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army) were close enough to assist, originally it was planned to coordinate with [Operation Bagration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Bagration), but was delayed. Some worried that people who did not participate would face collective punishment for the revolt. The Sonderkommando prisoners, who had been forced to work in the crematorium, were unwilling to wait; they worried they would be killed before the Red Army approached to [liberate the concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Auschwitz_concentration_camp), which eventually happened in 1945.[^cite_note-Sonderkommando-222]

### Allied forces during WWII

See also: [Suicide attack § Disposable use of troops during WWI and WWII](#Disposable_use_of_troops_during_WWI_and_WWII)

In 1941, some newspapers reported that, "While two New Zealand officers stood on a bridge in Greece holding up advancing Germans with their revolvers, a New Zealand sergeant placed two bared wires together and blew the bridge, the officers, and himself to smithereens".[^cite_note-223]

### Attacks by the Empire of Japan&#x20;

Further information: [Japanese Special Attack Units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Special_Attack_Units), [Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze), [ Kaiten (suicide torpedo)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaiten), [Kairyū-class submarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kairy%C5%AB-class_submarine), [Yokosuka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokosuka), [ Yokosuka MXY-7 Ohka (aircraft)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokosuka_MXY-7_Ohka), [Lunge mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunge_mine), [ Shinyo (motorboat)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinyo_\(suicide_motorboat\)), [Fukuryu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fukuryu), [Banzai charge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banzai_charge), [ Smertnik (Russian slur)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smertnik), and [Suicide Cliff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_Cliff)





**Left:** *Kamikaze* was a reference to two typhoons that sank or dispersed [Kublai Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan)'s [invading fleets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasions_of_Japan). **Right:** *Kamikaze* damage to the destroyer [USS *Newcomb*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Newcomb).

The most overt and prolific use of suicide attacks during [World War II](32927.md) was by [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan). It remains the most intense and deadly, campaign of suicide attacks in history.[^cite_note-IWM_transcript-4] [Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze) pilots on [suicide missions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission) flew aircraft full of bombs and [improvised missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improvised_explosives) at enemy targets.[^cite_note-IWM_transcript-4] The attacks used 2600 aircraft to kill 7000 allied naval personnel and 4000 Japanese suicide operatives.[^cite_note-IWM_transcript-4][^cite_note-IWM_kamikaze_failed-224]

*[Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)* was a ritual act of self-sacrifice carried out by Japanese pilots of explosive-laden [aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aircraft) against Allied warships which occurred on a large scale at the end of World War II. About 3000 attacks were made and about 50 ships were sunk.[^cite_note-225]

Later in the war, as Japan became more desperate, this act became formalised and ritualised. Planes were outfitted with explosives specific to the task of a suicide mission.[^cite_note-226] *Kamikaze* strikes were a weapon of [asymmetric war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymmetric_war) used by the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) against [United States Navy](20518076.md) and [Royal Navy](26061.md) [aircraft carriers](2219.md), although the [armoured flight deck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_flight_deck) of the Royal Navy carriers diminished *kamikaze* effectiveness. Along with fitting existing aircraft with bombs, the Japanese also developed the *[Ohka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokosuka_MXY-7_Ohka)*, a purpose-built suicide aircraft that was air-launched from a carrying bomber and propelled to the target at high speed using rocket engines. The Japanese Navy also used piloted [torpedoes](31424.md) called *[kaiten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaiten)* (heaven shaker) on suicide missions. Although sometimes called [midget submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midget_submarine), these were modified versions of the unmanned torpedoes of the time and are distinct from the torpedo-firing midget submarines used earlier in the war, which were designed to [infiltrate](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Infiltration) [shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shore) defenses and return to a [mother ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_ship) after firing their torpedoes. Although extremely hazardous, these midget submarine attacks were not technically suicide missions, as the earlier midget submarines had escape hatches. *Kaitens*, however, provided no means of escape.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228]

## Pilot suicides after WWII&#x20;

Further information: [§ List of suicide attacks during the Cold War](#List_of_suicide_attacks_during_the_Cold_War)

Some passenger [airline pilots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airline_pilots) appear to have committed [mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_murder) [murder-suicide](1468613.md), or attempted to, with no apparent political motives.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231]

### Examples of undisputed pilot suicides and attempted suicides

For a more comprehensive list, see [Suicide by pilot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_by_pilot).

- [Japan Air Lines Flight 350](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Air_Lines_Flight_350) – 1982 deliberate crash in Tokyo, Japan.

- [Germanwings Flight 9525](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germanwings_Flight_9525) – Suicidal co-pilot locked the captain out of the cockpit and deliberately crashed the aircraft into the [French Alps](961317.md), killing 150.

  [^cite_note-232]

### Examples of speculated and disputed pilot suicides

- [SilkAir Flight 185](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SilkAir_Flight_185) – 1997 aircraft crash in Indonesia

- [EgyptAir Flight 990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EgyptAir_Flight_990) – 1999 aircraft crash in the Atlantic Ocean

- [Malaysia Airlines Flight 370](42142305.md) (8 March 2014) – Pilot suicide is one of several competing explanations suggested for the disappearance of Malaysia Airlines Flight 370.
