After the [Surrender of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrender_of_Japan), at the [end of World War II in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_World_War_II_in_Asia), there was a massive reduction in the number of suicide attacks. The few that did occur were sometimes carried out by individuals for [personal reasons](#personal) such as [pilot suicides in commercial aircraft](#Pilot_suicides) (see above). For most of the [Cold War](325329.md) suicide attacks were only sporadic, but suicide attacks began to become more frequent towards the [end of the Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_the_Cold_War) ([see below](#1980s)).

### Insurgency in Palestine (1944–1948)&#x20;

See also: [Cold War (1947–1948)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1947%E2%80%931948\))

Further information: [ Insurgency in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Palestine) and [ Executed Irgun and Lehi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olei_Hagardom)

In the 1930s, Zionist insurgents in Palestine used [proxy bombing](#proxy_bombers) and [animal-borne bomb attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal-borne_bomb_attacks) in [their attacks on civilian targets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Irgun_attacks) such as [Haifa](55606.md) vegetable market.[^cite_note-Haaretz_20_June_1939-233][^cite_note-Persico-234] By the 1940s they explored the possibility of using humans on their own side, resulting in at least two deaths.[^cite_note-Margalit-235][^cite_note-Sheleg_2007-48] The [Lehi militant group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_\(militant_group\)) used [Judges 16\:30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judges_16\:30) in discussions about potential suicide attacks. In a meeting about ways to assassinate General [Evelyn Barker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evelyn_Barker), the British Army commander in [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine), a [young woman volunteered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber) to do the assassination as a [suicide bombing](18835454.md).[^cite_note-Margalit-235] They referred to it as a "[Let my soul die with the Philistines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Let_my_soul_die_with_the_Philistines)" proposal ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): תמות נפשי עם פלשתים), or a "[Samson option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson_option)".[^cite_note-Margalit-235][^cite_note-Judges_16-53] On that occasion other members of the group allegedly rejected her offer.[^cite_note-MargalitDeath-237] However, the Lehi collaborated with the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) militant group on at least one joint suicide bombing carried out by one militant from each group during the [insurgency against the British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Palestine) (before the [1948 Palestine war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestine_war)).[^cite_note-Charters_2007-238][^cite_note-Sheleg_2007_suicide_operation-55] The plan, named *Operation Samson* ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): מבצע שמשון, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Hebrew): *Mivtsa Shimshon*), was intended to be a suicide attack, but the two militants were [the only casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission#Failed_and_aborted_suicide_attacks).[^cite_note-Sheleg_2007-48][^cite_note-haaretz_OS-239][^cite_note-OS_161618-240] A Lehi militant and an Irgun militant blew themselves up in [Jerusalem Central Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Underground_Prisoners).[^cite_note-hangman_defeated-241][^cite_note-Mercury_1947-242][^cite_note-ih_2021-05-21-243][^cite_note-Charters_2007-238] They used [improvised grenades](265112.md) that had been constructed by another Lehi prisoner. The explosives were disguised as oranges to hide them from the guards, and smuggled in with the prisoners' food.[^cite_note-Srugim_Philistines-244][^cite_note-Perth_22_April_1947-245][^cite_note-Sheleg_2007_suicide_operation-55][^cite_note-ih_2021-05-21-243][^cite_note-Maariv_3_September_2023-246] The plan was to use the grenades as they were taken to the gallows to kill themselves, the executioners, and British officials who attended hangings.[^cite_note-Srugim_Philistines-244][^cite_note-Sheleg_2007-48][^cite_note-toi_2013-247] But the explosives detonated early, while the two of them were alone together in their cell.[^cite_note-Sheleg_2007-48] Allegedly when the pair learned that [Rabbi Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbi_Jacob_Goldman?action=edit\&redlink=1) would be present at their hanging, they changed the plan and committed suicide alone together before they were scheduled to be taken to the gallows.[^cite_note-Eretz_Modern_Era-248][^cite_note-252] The Lehi militant who built the IEDs, later had a leadership role in the Israeli military's [nuclear, chemical, and biological](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBRN_defense) [weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction) division ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): אב״כ).[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-ABC-258][^cite_note-gas-261] The story of their deaths has featured in multiple speeches by [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) leaders.[^cite_note-LS-266] In 2007, [The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post) described the double suicide as "One of the best-known stories of heroism leading to the creation of the State of Israel".[^cite_note-Pomerantz-267]

The prison is now the [Museum of Underground Prisoners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Underground_Prisoners)&#x20;

[^cite_note-268]

- Eight militants were hanged.

  Eight militants [were hanged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olei_Hagardom).

  [^cite_note-Dov-270]

- The intended location of the suicide attack was the gallows.

  The intended location of the suicide attack was the gallows.

  [^cite_note-early-271]

- Death row cell with oranges.

  Death row cell with oranges.

  [^cite_note-272]

- Office of the warden, one of the intended victims.

  Office of the warden, one of the intended victims.

  [^cite_note-toi_2013-247]

### Korean War (1950–1953)&#x20;

Further information: [Korean War](16772.md) and [Cold War (1948–1953)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1948%E2%80%931953\))

[North Korean](21255.md) tanks were attacked by [South Koreans](27022.md) with suicide tactics during the [Korean War](16772.md).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274]

American tanks in Seoul were attacked by North Korean suicide squads, who used satchel charges.[^cite_note-275] North Korean soldier [Li Su-Bok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Su-Bok?action=edit\&redlink=1) is considered a hero for destroying an American tank with a suicide bomb.[^cite_note-276]

In 1952, three Chinese soldiers reportedly killed themselves with hand grenades to avoid capture.[^cite_note-tr_49008320-100]

### 1953 in West Virginia&#x20;

See also: [§ NZ 1905](#NZ_1905), [§ USA 1927](#USA_1927), [§ China 1981](#China_1981), [1976 Mason County jail bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Mason_County_jail_bombing), [ 1959 Poe Elementary School bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poe_Elementary_School_bombing), and [Domestic violence in the United States](33778482.md)

In January 1953 at a magistrate's Court in [West Virginia](32905.md), 47-year-old Donzel McCray "turned himself into a human bomb" with sticks of dynamite strapped to his waist.[^cite_note-tr_194082329-277][^cite_note-280] He killed himself and injured his ex-wife and her lawyer.[^cite_note-tr_130926746-278][^cite_note-tr_175351189-281][^cite_note-tr_161665690-282][^cite_note-tr_194082329-277] The couple had six children and had divorced the previous September.[^cite_note-Nassau-61]

### Israeli and Egyptian wars (1956–1970)

Further information: [Six-Day War](29328.md), [Suicide attack § Nuclear](#Nuclear), and [Cold War (1953–1962)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1953%E2%80%931962\))

#### Early suicide tactics in Gaza

Some Israelis romanticize acts of self sacrifice in battle by analogy to the [Biblical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible) hero [Samson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson), particularly if they take place in [Gaza](55387.md), where Israelis believe he [committed suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samson#Death_by_suicide) and [killed thousands of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_with_the_Philistines) enemy [Philistines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philistines) in the process.[^cite_note-kikar-51] In situations where death or severe injury is already difficult to avoid, it is seen as heroic to abandon efforts to save one's self and instead focus on causing as much harm as possible to the enemy, in the process of effectively committing suicide.[^cite_note-yNet_Ali-52] This includes some anecdotes of events during their wars with Egypt.[^cite_note-kikar-51]

#### Suez Crisis (1956)&#x20;

Main articles: [Jules Jammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Jammal) and [Suez Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Crisis)

According to Egyptian media, during the [Suez Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Crisis) in 1956, an [Arab Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Christian) military officer from Syria, [Jules Jammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Jammal), sunk a French ship with a suicide attack.[^cite_note-283] However, none of the French ships named by the sources were harmed during the crisis. It is unclear which actual ship he is supposed to have sunk. One source calls the ship at issue the "[liner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_liner) *Jean D'Arc*"[^cite_note-284] and another the "French warship, *Jeanne D'Arc*".[^cite_note-285] There was a [French cruiser *Jeanne d'Arc*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_cruiser_Jeanne_d'Arc_\(1930\)) in service at that time, but it was decommissioned in 1964 rather than sunk. Some sources name the [battleship *Jean Bart*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_battleship_Jean_Bart_\(1940\)).[^cite_note-286]

#### "War of Attrition" (1967–1970)

See also: [Taysir Abu Saada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taysir_Abu_Saada), [War of Attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Attrition), [Battle of Karameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Karameh), and [Mosab Hassan Yousef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosab_Hassan_Yousef)

On 21 March 1968, in response to persistent [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) (PLO) raids against [Israelis](181296.md), Israel [attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Karameh) the town of [Karameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karameh), Jordan, the site of a major PLO camp. The goal of the invasion was to destroy the [Karameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karameh) [refugee camp](400347.md) and capture [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) in reprisal for the attacks by the PLO against Israelis. The PLO attacks had culminated in an Israeli school bus hitting a mine in the [Negev](142273.md).[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] According to a biography of an autobiography by [Tass Saada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tass_Saada?action=edit\&redlink=1) – an alleged former PLO sniper who [converted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ex-Muslims) to [Christianity in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_the_United_States) – this battle marked the [first known deployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks) of [suicide bombers by Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_bombers) ([Arab](#1947)) forces.[^cite_note-Once_an_Arafat_Man-289][^cite_note-Saada_Karameh-290] Modern academics describe aspects of the way the [Battle of Karameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Karameh) is remembered as a "myth" or "distortion".[^cite_note-291]

### Lod airport massacre (1972)&#x20;

Main articles: [Lod airport massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_airport_massacre), [Japanese Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Red_Army), [PFLP-EO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFLP-EO), and [Cold War (1962–1979)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1962%E2%80%931979\))

One of the first incidents to be labelled "suicide terrorism" was the [mass shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport_massacre) at the airport in [Lod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod), Israel's international airport.[^cite_note-292] Two of the attackers died during the attack, one of whom deliberately committed suicide using a hand grenade.[^cite_note-mTnbJ-293] According to [France 24](1263244.md) and [AFP](471981.md), "The massacre was planned as a suicide attack and all three Japanese militants had intended to mutilate their faces with their grenades to make [identification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_identification) more difficult".[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5]

It was carried out by three foreign fighters from the [Japanese Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Red_Army) (a communist militant group from Japan) in corroboration with the [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine – External Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine_%E2%80%93_External_Operations) ([PFLP-EO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFLP-EO)) division, led by [Wadie Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadie_Haddad), a rebellious offshoot of the [PFLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFLP).[^cite_note-Wadie_JC-294] Some reports at the time labelled the incident a "[Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)" attack,[^cite_note-col_rep-6] but others have criticized the label, including the surviving attacker's interpreter.[^cite_note-Satoshi_Sugawara-7] The Kamikaze were a [unit of suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Special_Attack_Units) in the airforce of [imperial Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Japan) in WWII, the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) had a very different ideology to the JRA. Researchers from [Duke University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_University) described the JRA's motives as "rooted in anti-imperialism, anti-colonialism, and anti-capitalism".[^cite_note-Global_Revolution-8] In 2010, [Ze'ev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ze'ev) Sarig, the former manager of [Lod Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport), compared the attack to the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) in New York, "This attack was for Israelis what the September 11th attacks were for Americans", when trying to sue North Korea for the attack in a United States court in [Puerto Rico](23041.md) in 2010.[^cite_note-mTnbJ-293]

### Cold War era literature and mythology

Further information: [Culture during the Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_during_the_Cold_War), [Suicide in literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_in_literature), and [History of suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_suicide)

#### The Revolt (1951)&#x20;





Left: Exhibit in the [Lehi Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_Museum) depicting [the prisoners](#1947) in red uniforms. Right: The most famous [Irgun attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Irgun_attacks), the 1946 [King David Hotel bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_David_Hotel_bombing), was not a suicide attack as such.

[^cite_note-295]

&#x20;However, many of the technical and propaganda techniques they used have now become ubiquitous in modern terrorism, including groups who have prolifically used suicide bombing.

[^cite_note-wotr-296]

[^cite_note-Davis_The_First_Car_Bomb-297]

[^cite_note-ICB_k35-298]

*[The Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Revolt)*, written by [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin) before he became [Prime Minister](24898.md), tells the story of the [Lehi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_\(militant_group\)) (referred to by their translated name's acronym, F.F.I. "[Freedom Fighters of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Fighters_of_Israel)" in early English editions) and Begin's own [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) fighters during the [Jewish insurgency in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Palestine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThe_Revolt-299] The Revolt influenced groups from a wide variety of ideologies.[^cite_note-WOTR_22_July_2021-300] Some of these groups were the groups began using suicide attacks in the 1990s. The book was allegedly found in [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden)'s [al-Qaeda](1921.md) organization.[^cite_note-WOTR_22_July_2021-300] Begin praised his militants' willingness to die, "These two wonderful young men greeted the sentence with the singing of [Hatikvah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatikvah)".[^cite_note-singing_of_Hatikvah-301] The "two wonderful young men" we're the two who blew themselves up [in 1947](#1947), he described their deaths, "even before they put on their red uniforms. They too held their heads high when they faced their judges… But they did not reach the gallows. They too sang on the threshold of death — a song of faith in God: 'Lord of the world who reigned before creation'. But their song ended with a great explosion which shattered the silence of the prison in occupied Jerusalem".[^cite_note-Lord_of_the_world-302][^cite_note-303] In 1977 the author became prime minister, after previously being in opposition.[^cite_note-1977_win-304] Historical militants featured prominently in his political speeches.[^cite_note-Begin_1964-305][^cite_note-1981_IAJBF-306][^cite_note-Tablet_IAJBW-307] He praised the actions of his militants during the [insurgency in Palestine in the 1940s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Palestine), including the leader of the cell who [bombed the King David Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_David_Hotel_bombing) (killing 91 people and 1 terrorist). His favourites were, again, the two young militants who blew themselves up in Jerusalem prison [in 1947](#1947).[^cite_note-1981_IAJBF-306][^cite_note-Tablet_IAJBW-307][^cite_note-Harkov_2019-308]

#### The Turner Diaries (1978)

Main article: [The Turner Diaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Turner_Diaries)

[The Turner Diaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Turner_Diaries) is a work of fiction, but is confirmed to have inspired terrorist attacks and far right extremist movements. In the final scene before the epilogue, the main character carries out a suicide attack on [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon), in an aircraft carrying a nuclear bomb.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] However, that specific scene is rarely linked to any specific attacks.

#### Preceding Cold War armed conflicts

Further information: [Cold War in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_in_Asia), [Soviet–Afghan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Afghan_War), [Islamic terrorism § 1960s and 1970s](566821.md#1960s_and_1970s), and [List of conflicts related to the Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conflicts_related_to_the_Cold_War)

[Sunni Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Muslims) were possibly the last major branch of the [Abrahamic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions) to resort to overt suicide attacks. Islamic suicide bombing is a fairly recent phenomenon. It was absent from the 1979–1989 [Afghan jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Afghan_War) against the Soviet Union,[^cite_note-FeldmanITNA-311] an asymmetrical war where the *mujahideen* fought [Soviet warplanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Air_Forces), helicopters and tanks primarily with light weapons. According to author [Sadakat Kadri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadakat_Kadri), "the very idea that Muslims might blow themselves up for God was unheard of before 1983, and it was not until the early 1990s that anyone anywhere had tried to justify killing innocent Muslims who were not on a battlefield". After 1983, the process was limited among Muslims to [Hezbollah](13919.md) and other [Lebanese Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims) factions for more than a decade.[^cite_note-Kadri-312]

The early 1980s saw an increase in the use of [terrorist tactics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Definition_of_terrorism) in the Middle East, by ideologically diverse groups. The book *[The Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Revolt)*, written by the former [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) commander [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin) before he became [Prime Minister](24898.md), influenced groups from a wide variety of ideologies, allegedly including [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden)'s [al-Qaeda](1921.md) organization.[^cite_note-WOTR_22_July_2021-300] In *[Rise and Kill First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rise_and_Kill_First)* Israeli journalist [Ronen Bergman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronen_Bergman) wrote that [Hezbollah](13919.md)'s 1983 campaign of coordinated [terrorist attacks](566821.md) against United States, French, and Israeli military installations in [Beirut](37428.md) drew inspiration from the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun)'s 1946 [bombing campaign against the British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Palestine) created an atmosphere of fear which eventually forced the British to withdraw.[^cite_note-Rise_Up_Book-313] Bergman further asserts that the influence of [Israeli-sponsored terrorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_state-sponsored_terrorism) operations on the emerging Islamists was also of operational nature: the Israeli proxy [Front for the Liberation of Lebanon from Foreigners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Lebanon_from_Foreigners) had carried out multiple deadly [truck bombings](155282.md) in Lebanon long before the [emergence of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hezbollah), a [Mossad](27619007.md) agent told Bergman: "I saw from a distance one of the cars blowing up and demolishing an entire street. We were teaching the Lebanese how effective a [car bomb](155282.md) could be. Everything that we saw later with Hezbollah sprang from what they saw had happened after these operations".[^cite_note-Rise_Up_Book-313]

### Increased suicide attacks in the 1980s

Further information: [Cold War in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_in_Asia), [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War), [Cold War (1979–1985)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1979%E2%80%931985\)), [Cold War (1985–1991)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War_\(1985%E2%80%931991\)), and [Islamic terrorism § 1980s and 1990s](566821.md#1980s_and_1990s)

#### Arabs began using suicide attacks in the 1980s



1983 suicide bombing of the [U.S. embassy in Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_US_embassy_bombing_in_Beirut).

The 1980s saw the first suicide attacks by Arabs, and by [stricter definitions of suicide attack](#suicide), the first suicide attacks in the Middle East. Earlier examples were the three Japanese foreign fighters [in 1972](#1972), only one of whom killed himself as planned, the [Ashkenazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meir_Feinstein) and [Kurdish-Iraqui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Barazani) duo who blew themselves up before reaching their target [in 1947](#1947) (see above).[^cite_note-1981_IAJBF-306][^cite_note-Tablet_IAJBW-307] As well as stories such as [Jules Jammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Jammal) whose suicide attacks probably happened only in myth. The [Islamic Dawa Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Dawa_Party)'s [car bombing of the Iraqi embassy in Beirut in December 1981](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_Iraqi_embassy_bombing) and [Hezbollah](13919.md)'s [bombing of the U.S. embassy in April 1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_1983_U.S._Embassy_bombing) and [attack on United States Marines and French barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Beirut_barracks_bombing) in October 1983 brought suicide bombings international attention and began the modern suicide bombing era.[^cite_note-314] Other parties to the civil war were quick to adopt the tactic, and by 1999 factions such as Hezbollah, the [Amal Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement), the [Ba'ath Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba'ath_Party), and the [Syrian Social Nationalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Social_Nationalist_Party) had carried out a total of roughly 50 suicide bombings. The Syrian Social Nationalist Party sent the [first recorded female suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sana'a_Mehaidli) in 1985.[^cite_note-FEMALE_SUICIDE_BOMBERS-315][^cite_note-Zedalis_google_books-316][^cite_note-Zedalis_martinfrost.ws-317][^cite_note-Rajan-318]

#### Hezbollah

Further information: [History of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hezbollah) and [Terrorism in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Lebanon)

One of the first bombing campaigns utilizing primarily suicide attacks had considerable political success. In the early 1980s, Hezbollah used these bombing attacks, targeting first foreign peacekeepers and then Israel. The result in both cases was the targets withdrawing from Lebanon.[^cite_note-319]

### Suicide bombings by individuals

Some of the first 1980s suicide bombings were by individuals, for both political and [personal](#personal) motives, for example:

#### 1981 Yangquan theatre bombing&#x20;

Main article: [Yangquan theatre bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangquan_theatre_bombing)

The Yangquan theatre bombing was a suicide bombing that killed 32 people, in a cinema in [Yangquan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangquan), [Shanxi](81262.md), [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_China), on 22 July 1981. On 22 July 1981, 24-year-old [Gao Haiping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gao_Haiping) invited a girl, who had dumped him previously, to a film screening in [Yangquan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangquan).[^cite_note-320] He intended to kill them both with a bomb made of about 3 kg of [ammonium nitrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ammonium_nitrate) and an electric detonator in a metal box. When the woman did not show up, Gao entered the theatre alone and took his place in seat 25 in the second row. At about 8 p.m., he put the bomb on his lap and detonated it, killing himself and 31 other people, wounding another 127, and severely damaging the theatre. Gao Haiping was an only child. He worked as a miner and was described as a loner who was pessimistic and dissatisfied with his life.[^cite_note-YTB_book-321] A suicide note and letters to his parents were found in a drawer in his home, as well as explosives wrapped in paper.[^cite_note-YTB_book-321][^cite_note-322]

#### Wanganui Computer Centre (1982)&#x20;

Main article: [Wanganui Computer Centre bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanganui_Computer_Centre_bombing)

See also: [Whanganui § History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whanganui#History), [Terrorism in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_New_Zealand), [Christchurch mosque shootings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christchurch_mosque_shootings), and [§ NZ 1905](#NZ_1905)

Not all [politically motivated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence) suicide attacks targeted other people. On 18 November 1982, [Neil Roberts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil_Roberts_\(Anarchist\)) carried out a [suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanganui_Computer_Centre_bombing#Bombing) in [Whanganui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whanganui), [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_New_Zealand).[^cite_note-323] His target was a facility housing the main computer centre of the [National Law Enforcement Database](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Law_Enforcement_System) belonging to [New Zealand Police](406756.md), [Courts](41422695.md), [Ministry of Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Transport_\(New_Zealand\)), and other law enforcement agencies, in [Whanganui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whanganui#History). The power of the explosion made it so that police were initially unable to determine the gender of the perpetrator.[^cite_note-324] The attacker, 22-year-old Neil Roberts, a "punk rock" [anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist), was the only person killed, and the computer system was undamaged.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] He had written on a piece of cardboard before the explosion, "Heres \[sic] one anarchist down. Hopefully there is a lot more waking up. One day we'll win – one day". A public toilet nearby had the slogan "We have maintained a silence closely resembling stupidity" painted on it, a slogan which the police believe Roberts had painted,[^cite_note-327] and borrowed from the Revolutionary Proclamation of the [Junta Tuitiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junta_Tuitiva) of 1809.[^cite_note-328] The phrase is still closely linked with the bombing by the New Zealand public.[^cite_note-329]

### Sri Lankan civil war

Further information: [Suicide bombings in Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bombings_in_Sri_Lanka)

During the [Sri Lankan civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lankan_civil_war), the [Tamil Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Tigers) (LTTE) adopted suicide bombing as a tactic, using bomb belts and [female bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber). The LTTE carried out their first suicide attack in July 1987.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] Their [Black Tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LTTE_Black_Tiger) unit committed 83 suicide attacks from 1987 to 2009, killing 981 people.[^cite_note-333] Those killed [included](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_assassinations_of_the_Sri_Lankan_Civil_War) former Indian Prime Minister [Rajiv Gandhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi)[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] and the president of Sri Lanka, [Ranasinghe Premadasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranasinghe_Premadasa).[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]

#### Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam (LTTE) 1987–2009

Further information: [Black Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Tigers)

The [Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) were thought to have mastered the use of [suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_attacks) and had a separate unit, "The [Black Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Tigers)", consisting "exclusively of cadres who have volunteered to conduct suicide operations".[^cite_note-341] The first prominent suicide bombing by the LTTE occurred in 1987 when [Captain Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_Miller_\(Tamil_militant\)) drove a [truck laden with explosives](155282.md) into a Sri Lankan army camp killing 40 soldiers.[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343]

Use of suicide by the LTTE had mixed results. The [Liberation Tigers of Tamil Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) (LTTE) pioneered the use of suicide bombings against civilian and political targets. In 2000, Yoram Schweitzer called the LTTE "unequivocally the most effective and brutal terrorist organization ever to utilize suicide terrorism".[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16] Their struggle for an [independent](62450.md) state in the [North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Province,_Sri_Lanka) and [East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Province,_Sri_Lanka) of the island lasted for 26 years and led to the deaths of two heads of state or government, several ministers, and up to 100,000 combatants and civilians, from by a [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN) estimate.[^cite_note-344] Politically, its attacks succeeded in halting the deployment of the Indian peace keeping troops to Sri Lanka and the subsequent postponement of the peace-talks in Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16] Nonetheless, the conflict ended in May 2009 not with an independent [Eelam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Eelam), but with the overrunning of LTTE strongholds and the killing of its leadership by the Sri Lankan military and security forces.

## Post-Cold War and war on terror

| Globe icon. | The examples and perspectives in this section **deal primarily with the [West Bank](33209.md) and the [Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant) and do not represent a [worldwide view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:WikiProject_Countering_systemic_bias) of the subject**. The specific issue is: **some details could be moved to [Palestinian suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_attacks).** You may [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Suicide_attack), discuss the issue on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Suicide_attack), or create a new section, as appropriate. *(July 2025)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
