### Post-Cold War Palestine

Further information: [ Palestinian suicide attacks (1989 to 2019)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_attacks), [ List of attacks (1989 to 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks), [Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intifada), [First Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Intifada), [Second Intifada](71717.md), and [ prisoner hunger strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger_strikes_by_Palestinian_prisoners_of_Israel)



Scene after a Palestinian suicide bombing in 1995.



Wreckage vehicles after a 2001 suicide bombing in Beit Lid Junction

[Suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks) became a popular tactic among [Palestinian militant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_terror) organizations such as [Hamas](13913.md), [Islamic Jihad](13093253.md), the [Al-Aqsa Martyrs Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Martyrs_Brigade), and occasionally by the [PFLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedahzur200466–69-362] The first suicide bombing in post-independence Israel was done by "Hamas" in 1994.[^cite_note-rwkckb07-363] Attacks peaked from 2001 to 2003 with over 40 bombings and over 200 killed in 2002.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365] Bombers affiliated with these groups often use so-called "[suicide belts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_belt)", [explosive devices](445224.md) which often included [shrapnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrapnel_\(fragment\)) designed to be strapped to the body under clothing. To maximize the loss of life, the bombers seek out enclosed spaces, such as cafés or city [buses](4146.md) crowded with people at [rush hour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rush_hour).[^cite_note-366] Less common are military targets such as soldiers waiting for transport at the roadside. These bombings have had more popular support than in other Muslim countries. More [music videos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_videos) and announcements that promise [eternal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eternity) reward for suicide bombers can be found on Palestinian television, according to [Palestinian Media Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Media_Watch).[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] Israeli sources observed that Hamas, Islamic Jihad and Fatah operate "Paradise Camps", training children as young as 11 to become suicide bombers.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] In 2004, due to increased effectiveness in Israel's security measures and stricter checkpoint protocols, terrorist organizations began employing [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber) and [children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_child_suicide_bombers_by_Palestinian_militant_groups) more frequently as operatives, assuming that they would raise fewer suspicions and undergo less rigorous inspections.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372]

#### Islamic Resistance Movement (Hamas)

Further information: [History of Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hamas)

Islamic Resistance Movement (Hamas) and their militant wing [al-Qassam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qassam), were formed in the 1980s but avoided using suicide in their [political violence](2646043.md) until the following decade. The group have utilized suicide intermittently in specific situations.[^cite_note-Jeffrey_15_January_2009-373] Suicide attempts are a criminal offense in Palestine and many other majority Muslim countries.[^cite_note-5_October_2023-27][^cite_note-decrim_countries-28][^cite_note-9_September_2021-29][^cite_note-unitedgmh-30][^cite_note-countries_afghanistan-25]

[Hamas](13913.md)'s most sustained suicide bombing campaign from 2003 to 2004 involved several members of Hebron's Masjad (mosque) al-Jihad soccer team. Most lived in the Wad Abu Katila neighborhood and belonged to the al-Qawasmeh *hamula* (clan). Several were classmates in the neighborhood's local branch of the Palestinian Polytechnic College. Their ages ranged from 18 to 22. At least eight team members were dispatched to suicide shooting and bombing operations by the Hamas military leader in Hebron, Abdullah al-Qawasmeh. Al-Qawasmeh was killed by Israeli forces in June 2003 and succeeded by his relatives Basel al-Qawasmeh, killed in September 2003, and Imad al-Qawasmeh, captured on 133 October 2004. In retaliation for the assassinations of Hamas leaders Sheikh [Ahmed Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Yassin) on 22 March 2004 and [Abdel Aziz al-Rantissi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Aziz_al-Rantissi) on 17 April 2004, Imad al-Qawasmeh dispatched Ahmed al-Qawasmeh and Nasim al-Ja'abri for a suicide attack on two buses in Beer Sheva. The attack took place on 31 August 2004. In December 2004, Hamas declared a halt to suicide attacks.[^cite_note-ATRAN-374]

On 15 January 2008, the son of [Mahmoud al-Zahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud_al-Zahar), the leader of Hamas in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md), was killed. Another son had been killed in a 2003 assassination attempt on Zahar. Three days later, Israel Defense Minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) ordered the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) to seal all border crossings with Gaza, cutting off the flow of supplies to the territory in an attempt to stop rocket barrages on Israeli border towns. Nevertheless, violence from both sides only increased. On 4 February 2008, friends Mohammed Herbawi and Shadi Zghayer, who were members of the Masjad al-Jihad soccer team, staged a suicide bombing at a commercial center in Dimona, Israel. Herbawi had previously been arrested as a 17-year-old on 15 March 2003 shortly after a suicide bombing on Haifa bus, which was done by Mamoud al-Qawasmeh on March 5, 2003. Herbawi had coordinated suicide shooting attacks on Israeli settlements by others on the team, such as on 7 March 2003 with an attack by Muhsein, Hazem al-Qawasmeh, Fadi Fahuri, and Sufian Hariz. He was also involved with another set of suicide bombings in Hebron and Jerusalem on 17 and 18 May 2003 by Fuad al-Qawasmeh, Basem Takruri, and Mujahed al-Ja'abri. Although Hamas claimed responsibility for the [Dimona attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Dimona_suicide_bombing), the politburo leadership in Damascus and Beirut was initially unaware of who initiated and carried out the attack. It appears that Ahmad al-Ja'abri, military commander of Hamas's [Izz ad-Din al-Qassam Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izz_ad-Din_al-Qassam_Brigades) in Gaza, requested the suicide attack through Ayoub Qawasmeh, Hamas's military liaison in Hebron, who knew where to look for eager young men who had self-radicalized together and had already mentally prepared themselves to die.[^cite_note-ATRAN-374][^cite_note-375]

#### Results of Palestinian suicide attacks



Early Israeli construction of West Bank barrier in 2003.

See also: [public diplomacy of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_diplomacy_of_Israel) and [Bulldozer politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulldozer_politics)

|  | This section **may be [too long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Section#Section_size_policies) or excessively detailed.** Please consider summarizing the material. *(January 2026)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

It is more difficult to determine whether Palestinian suicide bombings have proved to be a successful political tactic. Hamas "came to prominence" after the first intifada as "the main Palestinian opponent of the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords)", the US-sponsored peace process that oversaw the gradual and partial removal of Israel's occupation in return for Palestinian guarantees to protect Israeli security.[^cite_note-BBC-Hamas-2009-376] according to the BBC.[^cite_note-BBC-Hamas-2009-376][^cite_note-377] The accords were sidetracked after the 1996 election of right-wing Israeli leader [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md). Hamas's suicide bombings of Israeli targets "were widely" credited for the popularity among Israelis of the hardline Netanyahu, from Menachem Begin's [Likud party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud_party).[^cite_note-BBC-Hamas-2009-376]

The drop in suicide bombings in Israel has been explained by the many security measures taken by the Israeli government,[^cite_note-Schweitzer-2010-378][^cite_note-BBC-2007-379] especially the building of the "separation barrier",[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-HuffPost-2013-381] and a general consensus among Palestinians that the bombings were a "losing strategy".[^cite_note-HuffPost-2013-381] The suicides and other attacks on civilians had "a major impact" on the attitudes of the Israeli public.[^cite_note-Waxman-2011-382] Instead of creating demoralization, the attacks generated even greater support for the right-wing [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) party which brought to office another hardliner, the former general [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon). In 2001, 89% of Israeli Jews supported the Sharon government's policy of "targeted assassinations" of Palestinian militants involved in terrorism against Israel, the number rising to 92% in 2003.[^cite_note-Waxman-2011-382] Opinion polls of the Jewish Israelis found 78–84% supported the "[separation barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier)" in 2004.[^cite_note-383]

The "heightened security measures" also affected the target populations. During the bombing campaign, Israelis were questioned by armed guards and given a quick pat down before being let into cafés.[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003-384]

In the [West Bank](33209.md), the [IDF](39237.md) has at times [demolished homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_demolition_in_the_Israeli-Palestinian_conflict) that belong to families whose children or landlords whose tenants had volunteered for such missions, whether completed or not.[^cite_note-385] An internal review starting in October 2004 brought an end to the policy, but it was resumed in 2014.[^cite_note-386] Other military measures taken during the suicide attack campaign included: a widescale re-occupation of the West Bank and blockading of Palestinian towns; "targeted assassinations" of militants, an approach used since the 1970s; raids against militants suspected of plotting attacks; mass arrests; curfews; stringent travel restrictions; and physical separation from Palestinians via the 650 kilometres (400 mi) [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier) in and around the West Bank.[^cite_note-BBC-2007-379][^cite_note-387] The [Second Intifada](71717.md) and its suicide attacks are often dated as ending around the time of an unofficial ceasefire with some of the most powerful Palestinian militant groups in 2005.[^cite_note-BBC-2007-379] A new "[knife intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict_\(2015\))" started in September 2015. Still, although many Palestinians were killed in the process of stabbing or attempting to stab Israelis, their deaths were not "a precondition for the success" of their mission and so are not considered suicide attacks by many observers.[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16]

### Kurdistan Workers' Party (PKK)&#x20;

Main article: [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md)

See also: [History of the Kurdistan Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Kurdistan_Workers'_Party), [Turkish nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_nationalism), [Anfal campaign](802340.md), [Barzani (tribe)](4198200.md), and [Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga)



&#x20;PKK supporters at 2003 march opposing the [Iraq War](5043324.md), London

The [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) (PKK), a secular group, have also been involved in suicide attacks. Their first militant action was an unsuccessful attempt to assassinate [Mehmet Celal Bucak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Celal_Bucak?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-Joost-388]

The PKK began their insurgency against the Turkish state in 1984. However, they did not carry out their first suicide attack until the 1990s.

According to the [Chicago Project on Security and Terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Project_on_Security_and_Terrorism)'s [Suicide Attack Database](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_Attack_Database), as of 2015, ten suicide attacks by the PKK from 1996 to 2012 killed 32 people and injured 116.[^cite_note-389]

On 12 May 2025, the Kurdistan Workers' Party announced it would disband as part of a peace initiative with Turkey.[^cite_note-390]

### Al-Qaeda begin using suicide in the 1990s

[Al-Qaeda](1921.md) carried out its first suicide attack in the mid-1990s.[^cite_note-rwkckb07-363]



&#x20;The [U.S. Embassy in Dar es Salaam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Dar_es_Salaam), Tanzania, in the aftermath of 7 August 1998, [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) [suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_United_States_embassy_bombings).

### Start of the 21st Century&#x20;

In early 2000, analyst [Yoram Schweitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoram_Schweitzer) saw a pause in bombing campaigns and argued that "most of the groups that were involved in suicide terrorism either stopped using it or eventually reduced it significantly".[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16]

#### Narrowing definition of suicide attack

The number of attacks using suicide tactics grew from an average of fewer than five per year during the 1980s to 81 suicide attacks in 2001 and 460 in 2005.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006129-391] By 2005, the tactic had spread to dozens of countries.[^cite_note-392] The absolute number increased despite a narrowing of the definition of suicide attacks. The [United States government](195149.md) defined "modern" suicide bombing has been defined as "involving explosives deliberately carried to the target either on the person or in a civilian vehicle and delivered by surprise".[^cite_note-393] [Noah Feldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noah_Feldman)'s definition excludes attacks, such as the [Lod Airport massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport_massacre) ([see above](#Lod_airport_massacre_\(1972\))), where "the perpetrator's ensured death" was not "a precondition for the success of his mission"[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16] (despite the [Lod Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod_Airport) attack being overtly planned as a suicide attack, and often referred to as such,[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5] as well as the surviving attacker wanting the death penalty for himself).[^cite_note-Okamoto_hope-9]

### Russia and Chechnya

See also: [terrorism in Russia](8743696.md)

Suicide bombings have become a tactic in [Chechnya](6095.md), first being used in the conflict in 2000 in [Alkhan Kala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alkhan_Kala?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedahzur2004112-394] and spreading to Russia, notably with the [Moscow theater hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_theater_hostage_crisis) in 2002 and the [Beslan school hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beslan_school_hostage_crisis) in 2004.[^cite_note-395]

### 2001 September 11 attacks and after

Further information: [September 11 attacks](5058690.md), [Heather Penney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heather_Penney), and [War on terror](13425800.md)



The four hijacker-pilots who deliberately crashed the planes as part of the September 11 attacks. Left to right: Mohamed Atta, Marwan al-Shehhi, [Hani Hanjour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hani_Hanjour), [Ziad Jarrah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ziad_Jarrah)

The [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) in 2001, orchestrated by [al-Qaeda](1921.md), were the deadliest attacks on American soil since the Japanese [attack on Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor) which thrust the United States into [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-396] They involved the hijacking of four large passenger [jet airliners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jet_airliners). Unlike earlier airline hijackings, the primary focus was the planes instead of the passengers because their long transcontinental flight plans meant they carried more fuel, allowing a bigger explosion on impact.[^cite_note-lewis-2013-60] [American Airlines Flight 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_11) and [United Airlines Flight 175](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_175) were deliberately flown into the Twin Towers of the [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) in [New York City](645042.md), destroying both 110-story skyscrapers in less than two hours. [American Airlines Flight 77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Flight_77) was flown into [the Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) ([U.S. Department Of Defense](7279897.md) Headquarters) in [Arlington County, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington_County,_Virginia), causing severe damage to the west side of the building. These attacks resulted in the deaths of 221 people (including the 15 hijackers) on board the three planes as well as 2,731 more in and around the targeted buildings.[^cite_note-397] [United Airlines Flight 93](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines_Flight_93) crashed into a field near [Shanksville, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanksville,_Pennsylvania) after a revolt by the plane's passengers, killing all 44 people (including the four hijackers) on board.[^cite_note-NPR_3_September_2021-398][^cite_note-24_August_2021-399][^cite_note-Flight_93_National_Memorial-400] In total, the attacks killed [2,996 people and injured more than 6,000 others](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_September_11_attacks).[^cite_note-Thousands-401] The U.S. stock market closed for four trading days after the attacks in the first unscheduled close since the Great Depression.[^cite_note-402] Nine days after the attack, [U.S. President](24113.md) [George W. Bush](3414021.md) called for a "[war on terror](13425800.md)".[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] Shortly thereafter he launched the [War in Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Afghanistan_\(2001%E2%80%93present\)) to find and capture [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden), the leader of [al-Qaeda](1921.md).[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] A copy of [The Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Revolt), memoir of Irgun commander [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin), was found in one of [al-Qaeda](1921.md)'s training bases.[^cite_note-wotr-296][^cite_note-Hoffman_19_March_2015-410]

#### United States response

Further information: [Counterterrorism](83392.md), [Heather Penney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heather_Penney), [United States Air Force](32090.md), [Guantanamo Bay hunger strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guantanamo_Bay_hunger_strikes), and [public diplomacy of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_diplomacy_of_the_United_States)

After the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md), at least in the short term, the results were negative for Al-Qaeda, as well as the [Taliban](30635.md). Since the attacks, Western nations have diverted massive resources towards stopping similar actions, as well as increasing [border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border) security, and military actions against various countries believed to have been involved with terrorism.[^cite_note-411] Critics of the [war on terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_on_terrorism) suggest the results were negative, as the subsequent actions of the United States and other countries has increased the number of recruits and their willingness to carry out suicide bombings.

In the U.S., the post-9/11 era meant "previously inconceivable security measures—in airports and other transportation hubs, hotels and office buildings, sports stadiums and concert halls".[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003-384]

In the United States, the element of suicide in the 9/11 attacks persuaded many that previously unthinkable, "out of the box" strategic policies in a "[war on terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_on_terrorism)" were necessary. This included "preventive war" against countries not immediately attacking the U.S., to almost unlimited surveillance of virtually any person in the United States by the government without normal congressional and judicial oversight.[^cite_note-NPR-USTaHTOSI-412] These responses "produced their own costs and risks—in lives, national debt, and America's standing in the world".[^cite_note-NPR-USTaHTOSI-412]

### United States invasion of Iraq



&#x20;The result of a car bombing in Iraq.

After the [invasion of Iraq in 2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Iraq_in_2003) led by the U.S., [Iraqi and foreign insurgents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_insurgency_\(Iraq_War\)) carried out waves of suicide bombings. More attacks have been carried out in Iraq than in any other country, with 1,938 as of mid-2015.[^cite_note-CPOST-LOC-total-413]

In addition to [United States military](32212.md) targets, they attacked many civilian targets such as [Shiite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiite) [mosques](19894.md) as well as international offices of the [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN) and the [Red Cross](15487.md). Iraqi men waiting to apply for jobs with the new army and [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police) force were targets. In the lead up to the [Iraqi parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_parliamentary_election,_January_2005) on 30 January 2005, suicide attacks upon civilian and police personnel involved with the [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election) increased. There were also reports of the insurgents co-opting disabled people as involuntary suicide bombers.[^cite_note-414]

#### Islamic State in Iraq and Syria (ISIS)&#x20;

Further information: [Islamic State](9087364.md), [Islamic State in Iraq and Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_in_Iraq_and_Syria), [Islamic State in Iraq and the Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State_in_Iraq_and_the_Levant), and [Islamic State – Khorasan Province](48029263.md)

The self-declared "[Islamic State](9087364.md)" (ISIS)[^cite_note-418] use suicide attacks against government targets before they attack. The attackers use a wide range of methods, from suicide vests and belts to bomb trucks and cars and APCs filled with explosives. Usually, the suicide bomber involved in a "martyrdom operation" will record his last words in a martyrdom video before they start their attack, which will be released after the suicide attack is done. A study published by [The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian) in 2017 analyzed 923 attacks done between December 2015 and November 2016 and compared the military tactic to those used by *kamikaze* operations.[^cite_note-guardian2017-419] [Charlie Winter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Winter?action=edit\&redlink=1), the author of the study, said that ISIS had "industrialized the concept of martyrdom". 84% of suicide attacks were directed towards military targets, usually with armed vehicles. About 80% of the attackers were of Iraqi or Syrian origin.[^cite_note-guardian2017-419] According to the [Institute for National Security Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_National_Security_Studies_\(Israel\)), there were fewer suicide attacks worldwide in 2017, but more female suicide bombers participated in them. According to the institute, ISIS and al-Qaeda led the suicide terrorism.[^cite_note-420]

In 2017 and 2019, during the [Sinai insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_insurgency), there were suicide bombings in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) by local ISIS sympathizers. ISIS are a global extremist group, with an ideology that fundamentally [opposes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_and_nationalism) the [Palestinian](47246228.md) [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_nationalism) of Hamas and the other groups above.[^cite_note-anti-421] In 2017 two [Hamas government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_in_the_Gaza_Strip) border guards were killed while attempting to intercept an ISIS suicide bomber at [Rafah Crossing](10475618.md).[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-npr.org-423] The Hamas government responded to that bombing with a crackdown on followers of "[deviant ideologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideology_of_the_Islamic_State)" (meaning ISIS and [similar groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_anti-Hamas_groups_in_the_Gaza_Strip)).[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] In 2018, members of [ISIS in the Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Province) "declared war" on Hamas, demanding Hamas release ISIS militants held in [Gaza's prisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-426] Then in 2019, another suicide attack – also attributed to ISIS – directly targeted [Gaza Strip police](34888928.md).[^cite_note-427] Three police officers were killed,[^cite_note-reuters.com-428][^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] all three victims were allegedly members of Hamas.[^cite_note-npasyria.com-431] Gaza's Security forces responded by arresting ten people whom they suspected were members of the cell who arranged the attack.

In the following years, ISIS members also carried out suicide attacks in different locations. In December 2018, according to the director of the [Syrian Observatory for Human Rights](34029669.md), [Rami Abdel Rahman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rami_Abdul_Rahman), at least three suicide bombers blew themselves up inside the city of [As-Suwayda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/As-Suwayda) in the [Jabal al-Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jabal_al-Druze) of southern [Syria](7515849.md). This was in addition to suicide bombers who attacked seven villages in the surrounding suburbs.[^cite_note-432] According to [The Meir Amit Intelligence and Terrorism Information Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Meir_Amit_Intelligence_and_Terrorism_Information_Center), In January 2019, ISIS carried out a suicide bombing attack using a car bomb against a joint American-[Kurdish](3979351.md) patrol.[^cite_note-433] [2021 Kabul airport attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Kabul_airport_attack) was suicide bombing attack. In January 2021, ISIS claimed responsibility for a double suicide bombing in a [Baghdad](4492.md) market that killed at least 32 people and wounded more than 100. It was the first major suicide attack by the Islamic State group in the previous three years.[^cite_note-434] In June 2025, the Syrian Interior Ministry announced a suicide attack carried out by a member of the Islamic State cult in [Church](6325.md). According to the statement, he shot at worshippers in a church and then blew himself up inside.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436]

### South and Central Asia

Main article: [Terrorism in Central Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Central_Asia)

#### Afghanistan

Further information: [United States invasion of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan)

Other major locations of suicide attack are Afghanistan, with 1,059 attacks as of mid-2015,[^cite_note-CPOST-LOC-total-413] and Pakistan, with 490 attacks.[^cite_note-CPOST-LOC-total-413] In the first eight months of 2008, Pakistan overtook Iraq and Afghanistan in suicide bombings, with 28 bombings killing 471 people.[^cite_note-437]

#### Pakistan

Further information: [terrorism in Pakistan](2816470.md) and [public diplomacy of Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_diplomacy_of_Pakistan)

Shaheed (martyr) [Benazir Bhutto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benazir_Bhutto), former [Prime Minister of Pakistan](461896.md) and leader of the [Pakistan People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_People's_Party) (PPP), was [assassinated in a terrorist attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Benazir_Bhutto) on 27 December 2007.[^cite_note-injury-36][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-Bhutto_survived-439] Benazir and 23 other people were killed by a 16-year-old [suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bomber) using an [explosive belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_belt) and [used a gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_law_in_Pakistan).[^cite_note-exhumation-37] Bhutto had already survived a previous [assassination attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Karsaz_bombing) in [Karachi](17123.md).[^cite_note-Bhutto_survived-439][^cite_note-nytimes_karachi-440][^cite_note-441] Following this, many schools and universities were named in honour of her martyrdom.[^cite_note-Benazir-31]

### Africa

Further information: [Terrorism in Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Africa?action=edit\&redlink=1)

Since 2006, [al-Shabaab](8726463.md) has carried out major suicide attacks in [Somalia](3070332.md),[^cite_note-442] the worst year so far being 2016 with 28 attacks.

### Western Europe

See also: [terrorism in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Europe)

In Europe, four [Islamist suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamist_suicide_bombers?action=edit\&redlink=1) exploded home-made peroxide explosives on three London underground trains and a bus on [7 July 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_July_2005_London_bombings), during the morning rush hour. These "7/7" bombings killed 52 civilians and injured 700.[^cite_note-443]

On 22 May 2017, the [Manchester Arena bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_Arena_bombing) occurred which resulted in 23 deaths and 1,017 injuries. The attack was carried out as people were leaving an [Ariana Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariana_Grande) concert.[^cite_note-444]

### Car bomb suicides in the 2020s&#x20;

Further information: [Car bomb](155282.md), [Lone wolf terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lone_wolf_terrorism), [2020 Nashville bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Nashville_bombing), and [2025 Palm Springs fertility clinic bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Palm_Springs_fertility_clinic_bombing)

[On 25 December 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Nashville_bombing), a suicide bombing occurred in [Nashville, Tennessee](22018.md).

[On 17 May 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Palm_Springs_fertility_clinic_bombing), a 2010 silver [Ford Fusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Fusion_\(Americas\)) sedan loaded with explosives [detonated outside the American Reproductive Centers fertility clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Palm_Springs_fertility_clinic_bombing) in [Palm Springs, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Springs,_California), resulting in the death of the perpetrator, 25-year-old [Guy Edward Bartkus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Edward_Bartkus), and injuries to four others.[^cite_note-NBCNews1-445][^cite_note-Desert_Sun-446][^cite_note-nihilistic_ideations-447] Nearby buildings were damaged and windows were shattered.[^cite_note-FBI_calls_it-448][^cite_note-Springs_California-449] A [tripod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripod) and camera were found at the scene.[^cite_note-BalchanPost-450][^cite_note-nihilistic_ideations-447] The FBI called it an "intentional act of terrorism".[^cite_note-Desert_Sun-446][^cite_note-FBI_calls_it-448] Bartkus reportedly identified himself as the perpetrator of the bombing in a [video posted online](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_video), an audio recording, and [manifesto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manifesto) in which he described himself as a "pro-mortalist" and said people did not give consent to exist.[^cite_note-Suspect_identified-451] A 32-year-old man named Daniel Jongyon Park was arrested for possible involvement.[^cite_note-Washington_man-452][^cite_note-in_connection_with-453] Park was expelled from Poland to face prosecution on the United States.[^cite_note-Poland_Park-454]

## Attacker demographics&#x20;

### Children and teenagers

See also: [History of children in the military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_children_in_the_military) and [Use of child suicide bombers by Palestinian militant groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_child_suicide_bombers_by_Palestinian_militant_groups)



26 May 1945. Corporal [Yukio Araki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukio_Araki), holding a puppy, with four other pilots of the [72nd *Shinbu* Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/72nd_Shinbu_Squadron) at [Bansei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaseda,_Kagoshima), [Kagoshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kagoshima). Araki died the following day, at the age of 17, in a suicide attack on ships near [Okinawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okinawa_Island).

[Araki Yukio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukio_Araki) was born on 10 March 1928, at the age of fifteen he joined the [Imperial Japanese Army Air Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Japanese_Army_Air_Service)'s Youth Pilot Training Program.[^cite_note-kamikazeimages.net-455] On 27 May 1945, Araki took off from Bansei Airfield, in [Kagoshima Prefecture](217439.md), on a *[kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)* mission, flying a [Mitsubishi Ki-51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitsubishi_Ki-51).[^cite_note-Gordon-456] At the age of seventeen, Araki was one of the youngest known *kamikaze* pilots. It has been speculated that his plane was one of two that struck the USS *Braine*, killing 66 of her crew; however, the ship did not sink.[^cite_note-kamikazeimages.net-455][^cite_note-bos_carole-457]

There was a heated dispute surrounding the death penalty trial of the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) militant who [blew himself up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_mission) in 1947 ([see above](#1947)). He was sentenced to death alongside another militant for their role in the [bombing of Jerusalem Train Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Jerusalem_Train_Station?action=edit\&redlink=1), but the other militant later had his sentence commuted to life in prison.[^cite_note-Aderet_2021-11-01-458][^cite_note-MF_iz-459] There was heated debate about the age of the Irgun suicide militant when he was sentenced. His mother and brother claimed he was 17, too young to be executed according to the law of the British authorities.[^cite_note-MR_27_July_2017-47][^cite_note-JTA_18_Apr_47-460][^cite_note-JTA_4_April_1947-461][^cite_note-Benjamin-462][^cite_note-Bella-463] The court claimed he was 23, because he had served in the British military during World War II, and the British refused to believe they had recruited a minor who was lying about his age.[^cite_note-JTA_18_Apr_47-460] [Yehuda Lapidot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehuda_Lapidot) and the IDF say he was born on 5 October 1927.[^cite_note-Yehuda_Lapidot_-_the_Irgun-464] Surviving relatives disagree, maintaining that he was born in July 1929.[^cite_note-MR_27_July_2017-47]

[Mohammed Hossein Fahmideh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Hossein_Fahmideh), a 13-year-old Iranian boy who fought in the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War), is said – by former [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_activities_in_Iran) operative [Robert Baer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Baer) – to be the first Muslim to have participated in such an attack in contemporary history.[^cite_note-Robert_Baer-465] The boy strapped [rocket-propelled grenades](26286.md) to his chest and blew himself up under an Iraqi tank in November 1980. [Ayatollah Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah_Khomeini) declared Fahmideh a national hero.[^cite_note-tebyan_Mohammed_Hossein_Fahmideh-466] According to Robert Baer, the boy was used as an inspiration for further [volunteers for martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Willingness_to_die_in_battle).[^cite_note-Robert_Baer-465] The Iranian website, [Tebyan Cultural Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tebyan_Cultural_Institute) describes the child's death as heroic.[^cite_note-tebyan_Mohammed_Hossein_Fahmideh-466] According to former CIA officer Robert Baer, "[Ayatollah Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah_Khomeini)'s embattled Islamic republic adopted Fahmideh as a national hero and as an inspiration for further bloodshed and [martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Islam#Fahmideh)".[^cite_note-Robert_Baer-465]

In [Lebanon](17771.md) on 9 April 1985, [Sana'a Mehaidli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sana'a_Mehaidli), a 16-year-old member of the [Syrian Social Nationalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Social_Nationalist_Party) (SSNP), detonated an explosive-laden vehicle that killed two Israeli soldiers and injured twelve more. She is often described as the first [female suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber).[^cite_note-FEMALE_SUICIDE_BOMBERS-315][^cite_note-Zedalis_google_books-316][^cite_note-Zedalis_martinfrost.ws-317][^cite_note-Rajan-318] She is known as "the Bride of the South".[^cite_note-467] During the [Lebanese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War), female SSNP members bombed Israeli troops and the Israeli proxy militia, the [South Lebanon Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_Army).

[Dylan Klebold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dylan_Klebold) was 17 and [Eric Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Harris) was 18 when they carried out the [suicide attack](#1999) on [their highschool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbine_High_School_massacre).[^cite_note-NYT_25_April_1999-19][^cite_note-1999_updated_2011-20] It was originally planned as a bombing, ending in a double car bombing, but the bombs failed to detonate, so they shot their victims instead, then both shot themselves in the head.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469]

[Italian Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Australian) teenager [Jake Bilardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Bilardi) was 18 years old when he travelled to the Iraq to carry out a suicide attack for an ISIS-linked group.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] His attack targeted the Iraqi Army, who claimed they sustained no casualties. His death was used in ISIS propaganda.[^cite_note-Jake_SMH-474] The death of a young English-speaking foreigner also attracted far more mainstream media attention than most of the group's suicide attacks.[^cite_note-Jake_SMH-474] One Australian newspaper described the 18-year-old man as having been "groomed".[^cite_note-475]

[Talha Asmal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talha_Asmal), a 17-year-old, is the youngest suicide bomber in British history. He ran away from his home in [Dewsbury, West Yorkshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dewsbury) in March 2015 to join ISIS, and in June he participated in a suicide attack in Iraq, detonating a vehicle full of explosives.[^cite_note-\:8-476] He was part of a team of suicide bombers who targeted forces near an oil refinery south of [Baiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baiji_\(city\)). At least eleven people were reported to have been killed in the attack.[^cite_note-\:9-477] ISIS published photos of Talha taken right before the attack.[^cite_note-\:4-478] His parents stated they believed their son had been groomed and exploited by jihadists, whom they said deliberately targeted him in his "tender years and naivety." They urged others who fear their relatives might be becoming radicalized to contact the authorities.[^cite_note-\:92-479]

### Women&#x20;

This section is an excerpt from [Female suicide bomber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Female_suicide_bomber\&action=edit)]



A U.S. Navy servicewoman poses as a captured female suicide bomber during the [OPFOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OPFOR) exercise in [Pearl Harbor, Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Harbor,_Hawaii)

[Female suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_suicide_bomber) are women who undertake [suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_attacks). Suicide bombers are normally viewed as male political radicals, but since the [1960s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960s#Politics_and_wars), female suicide attacks have been on the rise. Through 1985–2006, 15% of all suicide attacks were conducted by female suicide bombers.[^cite_note-Female_suicide_bomber_\:0-480]

There are many organizations, such as [Boko Haram](23769768.md) (which is the first group to use women in a majority of their [suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bombings) and surpassed the [Tamil Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Tigers) in using more female suicide bombers than any other terrorist group in history),[^cite_note-Female_suicide_bomber_veconomist-481] [Al-Shabaab](8726463.md), [ISIS](9087364.md) and the [Al Aqsa Martyrs Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Aqsa_Martyrs_Brigade), that recently started using women as tools in their attacks, since they are normally viewed as less of a threat than their male counterparts. This includes women having the element of surprise, a hesitancy to search women, increased publicity for female suicide bombing attacks, and the female stereotype as non-violent.[^cite_note-482]

Research shows that female suicide bombers tend to replace depleted male cadres and tend to exhibit little active participation or inadequate training.[^cite_note-Female_suicide_bomber_\:8-483] An analysis of suicide attacks over the period 1985-2017 finds that female suicide bombers inflict fewer fatalities than their male counterparts.[^cite_note-Female_suicide_bomber_\:8-483]

#### Gendered motivations

In some traditions, women are customarily seen as peace-makers rather than as front-line actors in conflicts.[^cite_note-484] This stereotype has made them useful as suicide bombers, because they might be underestimated and thus be able to enter target areas inconspicuously, leading to more lethal suicide attacks.[^cite_note-485] Whether women's motivations for becoming suicide bombers generally differ from men's remains a pertinent question. Bloom has suggested some salient reasons for women to turn to suicide bombings, such as "to avenge a personal loss, to redeem the family name, to escape a life of sheltered monotony and achieve fame, or to equalize the patriarchal societies in which they live".[^cite_note-Bloom_2007-486] Some earlier literature suggested that women tend to be motivated by personal trauma rather than by ideological reasons.[^cite_note-Bloom_2007-486] Other researchers disagree with this assessment and state that it reduces women's political agency, seeing as they are just as capable of making a choice based on ideology.[^cite_note-487] Women's as well as men's usual motivations for becoming suicide bombers should be assumed to be nuanced and complex.[^cite_note-488]

## Tactics and strategies

### Devices

Further information on [suicide weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_weapons): [explosive belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_belt), [car bomb](155282.md), [improvised explosive device](265112.md), [Kaiten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaiten), [Kairyū-class submarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kairy%C5%AB-class_submarine), [Yokosuka MXY7 Ohka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokosuka_MXY7_Ohka), [Shin'yō-class suicide motorboat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shin'y%C5%8D-class_suicide_motorboat), [Fukuryu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fukuryu), and [Lunge mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lunge_mine)

Various groups adapt their strategies to suit specific targets. For example, in the 1980s, [Hezbollah](13919.md) favored the use of explosive-laden cars, while the [LTTE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam) in [Sri Lanka](26750.md) employed tactics involving explosive-laden boats. Palestinian organizations in the 1990s refined an approach involving suicide bombers with [explosive belts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive_belt), influencing groups like the [Chechens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chechens) and the [PKK](69680.md). In contemporary Iraq, local factions have utilized [explosive-laden vehicles](155282.md#History) to target heavily guarded military facilities.[^cite_note-Moghadam_2006-2] Decades earlier, the Lehi militant group were discovered to have constructed a "coat bomb", with explosives concealed by being stitched inside the coat.[^cite_note-Haaretz_coat_bomb-489] They appear to have attempted to use it in a non-suicide bombing, taking it off and leaving it behind in the targeted building, but it failed to detonate for unclear reasons.[^cite_note-Betty-490] They also used it for smuggling explosives into England.[^cite_note-Haaretz_coat_bomb-489]

### Strategic advantages

According to author [Jeffrey William Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_William_Lewis?action=edit\&redlink=1), success campaigns of suicide bombing require: willing individuals, organisations to train and use them, and a society willing to accept such acts in the name of a greater good.[^cite_note-lewis-2013-60] The organisations work to guarantee individual suicide bombers that they "will be remembered as martyrs dying for their communities". By imbuing suicide attacks with "reverence and heroism", it becomes more attractive to recruits.[^cite_note-lewis-2013-60] According to Yoram Schweitzer, modern suicide terrorism is "aimed at causing devastating physical damage, through which it inflicts profound fear and anxiety". Its goal is not to produce a negative psychological effect only on the victims of the actual attack, but on the entire target population.[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16] Attackers themselves have often framed suicide attacks as acts of courageous self-sacrifice made necessary by the superior military or security strength of the enemy. The technique has also been called "the atomic weapon of the weak".[^cite_note-Schweitzer-2010-378] According to [Hamas](13913.md) founder Sheikh [Ahmed Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Yassin), "Once we have warplanes and missiles, then we can think of changing our means of legitimate self-defense. But right now, we can only tackle the fire with our bare hands and sacrifice ourselves".[^cite_note-491]

A major reason for the popularity of suicide attacks, despite the sacrifice involved for its perpetrators, is its tactical advantages over other types of terrorism such as the ability to conceal weapons, make last-minute adjustments, an increased ability to infiltrate heavily guarded targets, and the lack of need for remote or delayed detonation, escape plans or rescue teams. Robert Pape observed that "Suicide attacks are an especially convincing way to signal the likelihood of more pain to come, because if you are willing to kill yourself you are also willing to endure brutal retaliation. \[...] The element of suicide itself helps increase the credibility of future attacks because it suggests that attackers cannot be deterred".[^cite_note-492] Other scholars have criticized Pape's research design, arguing that it cannot draw any conclusions on the efficacy of suicide terrorism.[^cite_note-Ashworth_2008-493]

[Bruce Hoffman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Hoffman) described the characteristics of suicide bombing as "universal" — "Suicide bombings are inexpensive and effective. They are less complicated and compromising than other kinds of terrorist operations. They guarantee media coverage. The suicide terrorist is the ultimate smart bomb. Perhaps most important, coldly efficient bombings tear at the fabric of trust that holds societies together".[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003-384]

Suicide bombings are often followed by heightened security measures and [reprisals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reprisal) by their targets. Because a deceased suicide bomber cannot be targeted, the response is often a targeting of those believed to have sent the bomber. Because the threat of retaliation cannot deter future attacks if the attackers were already willing to kill themselves, pressure is great to employ intensive surveillance of virtually any potential perpetrator, "to look for them almost everywhere, even if no evidence existed that they were there at all".[^cite_note-NPR-USTaHTOSI-412]

## Motivation for attacks

According to [Bruce Hoffman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Hoffman) (quoting the [RAND Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAND_Corporation)) and [Assaf Moghadam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assaf_Moghadam), suicide attacks distinguish themselves from other terror attacks due to their heightened lethality and destructiveness.[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003_&_RAND-494][^cite_note-Moghadam_2006-2] Perpetrators benefit from the ability to conceal weapons and make last-minute adjustments, and there is no need for escape plans or rescue teams. There is also no need to conceal their identities.[^cite_note-identities-495] In the case of suicide bombings, they do not require remote or delayed detonation.[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003-384] Although they accounted for only 4% of all "terrorist attacks" between 1981 and 2006, they resulted in 32% of terrorism-related deaths at 14,599 deaths. 90% of these attacks occurred in [Afghanistan](737.md), [Iraq](7515928.md), [Israel](9282173.md), [Palestine](241405.md), [Pakistan](23235.md), and [Sri Lanka](26750.md).[^cite_note-What_Motivates-496] By mid-2015, approximately three-quarters of all suicide attacks occurred in just three countries: [Afghanistan](15361156.md), [Pakistan](2816470.md), and [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Iraq).[^cite_note-CPOST-LOC-total-413]

William Hutchinson describes suicide attacks as a weapon of [psychological warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_warfare)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHutchinson2007-497] aimed at instilling fear in the target population,[^cite_note-Psychology_of_Terrorism_2014-498] undermining areas where [the public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_public) feels secure, and eroding the "fabric of trust that holds societies together." This weapon is further used to demonstrate the lengths perpetrators will go to achieve their goals.[^cite_note-Hoffman-6-2003-384] Motivations for suicide attackers vary. *[Kamikaze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamikaze)* pilots acted under military orders, while other attacks have been driven by [religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_terrorism) or [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalism) purposes. According to analyst [Robert Pape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Pape), prior to 2003, most attacks targeted occupying forces. For example, 90% of attacks in Iraq before the [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_conflict_\(2003%E2%80%93present\)) started in 2003 aimed at forcing out occupying forces. Pape's tabulation of suicide attacks runs from 1980 to early 2004 in [*Dying to Win*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dying_to_Win:_The_Strategic_Logic_of_Suicide_Terrorism), and to 2009 in [*Cutting the Fuse*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Pape#Cutting_the_Fuse).[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500] According to American-French anthropologist [Scott Atran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Atran), from 2000 to 2004, the ideology of Islamist [martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istishhad) played a predominant role in motivating the majority of bombers.[^cite_note-Atran_2006_page_127-501]

### Recent attacker profiles and motivations

Studies of who becomes a suicide attacker and what motivates them have often come to different conclusions. According to Riaz Hassan,[^cite_note-What_Motivates-496]

> apart from one demographic attribute—that the majority of suicide bombers tend to be young males—the evidence has failed to find a stable set of demographic, psychological, socioeconomic and religious variables that can be causally linked to suicide bombers' personality or socioeconomic origins.

Anthropologist [Scott Atran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Atran) wrote, "\[Terrorists] are not sufficiently different from everyone else. Insights into homegrown jihadi attacks will have to come from understanding group dynamics, not individual psychology. Small-group dynamics can trump individual personality to produce horrific behavior in otherwise ordinary people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006128-82] Atran's research has found that the attacks are not organized from the top down, but occur from the bottom up. It is usually a matter of following one's friends and ending up in environments that foster [groupthink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupthink). Atran is also critical of the claim that terrorists simply crave destruction; rather, they are often motivated by beliefs they hold sacred, as well as their moral reasoning.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006136-502]

A study of the remains of 110 suicide bombers in Afghanistan for the first part of 2007 by Afghan pathologist Yusef Yadgari found 80% were suffering from physical ailments such as missing limbs (before the blasts), cancer, or leprosy. Also, in contrast to earlier findings of suicide bombers, the Afghan bombers were "not celebrated like their counterparts in other Arab nations. Afghan bombers are not featured on posters or in videos as martyrs".[^cite_note-503] Menachem Begin called his only known suicide militant as "Meir the Stump", a reference to his amputated left arm.[^cite_note-Begin_May_1947-504]

[Robert Pape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Pape), director of the Chicago Project on Suicide Terrorism, found the majority of suicide bombers came from the educated middle classes. For example, [Humam Balawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humam_Khalil_Abu-Mulal_al-Balawi), who perpetrated the [Camp Chapman attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Chapman_attack) in Afghanistan in 2010, was a medical doctor.[^cite_note-505]

A 2004 paper by [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) Professor of Public Policy [Alberto Abadie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Abadie) "cast\[s] doubt on the widely held belief that terrorism stems from poverty, finding instead that terrorist violence is related to a nation's level of political freedom", with countries "in some intermediate range of political freedom" more prone to terrorism than countries with "high levels" of political freedom or countries with "highly authoritarian regimes". "When governments are weak, political instability is elevated, so conditions are favorable for the appearance of terrorism".[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507] A 2020 study found that while well-educated and economically well-off individuals are more likely to be behind suicide terrorism, it is not because these individuals self-select into suicide terrorism, but rather because terrorist groups are more likely to select high-quality individuals to commit suicide terrorist attacks.[^cite_note-508]

Pape found that among [Islamic suicide terrorists](566821.md), 97 percent were unmarried and 84 percent were male. If the [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) was excluded, this changed to be 91 percent male.[^cite_note-Pape-2003-80] A study conducted by the [U.S. military](32212.md) in [Iraq](7515928.md) in 2008 found that suicide bombers were almost always single men without children aged 18 to 30, with a mean age of 22, and were typically students or employed in [blue-collar occupations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue-collar_worker).[^cite_note-509] In a 2011 doctoral thesis, anthropologist Kyle R. Gibson reviewed three studies documenting 1,208 suicide attacks from 1981 to 2007 and found that countries with higher [polygyny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygyny) rates correlated with greater production of suicide terrorists.[^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511] Political scientists [Valerie M. Hudson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valerie_M._Hudson) and Bradley Thayer noted that countries where polygyny is widely practiced tend to have higher [homicide rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_intentional_homicide_rate) and [rates of rape](5672810.md). The pair have argued that because [Islam is the only major religious tradition where polygyny is still largely condoned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygyny_in_Islam), the higher degrees of marital inequality in [Islamic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world) compared to most of the world causes them to have larger populations susceptible to suicide terrorism. Hudson and Theyer contended that [promises of harems of virgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houri) for [martyrdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahid) serves as a mechanism to mitigate [in-group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-group_and_out-group) conflict within Islamic countries by redirecting their violence towards out-groups.[^cite_note-512]

Along with his research on the [Tamil Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tigers_of_Tamil_Eelam), Scott Atran found that Palestinian jihadist groups such as [Hamas](13913.md) provide monthly [stipends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stipend), [lump-sum payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lump_sum), and prestige to the families of suicide terrorists.[^cite_note-513][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006127-147-514] Cognitive scientist [Steven Pinker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Pinker) argues in *[The Better Angels of Our Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Better_Angels_of_Our_Nature)* (2011) that because the families of men in the [West Bank](33209.md) and [Gaza](12047.md) often cannot afford [bride prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bride_price) and that many potential brides end up in polygynous marriages, the financial compensation of an act of suicide terrorism can buy enough brides for a man's brothers to have children to make the self-sacrifice pay off in terms of [kin selection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kin_selection) and [biological fitness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fitness_\(biology\)).[^cite_note-515]

Motivations vary greatly and are different in the case of each individual. Fanaticism (nationalist, religious, or both) may result from brain-washing, negative experiences regarding "the enemy", and the lack of a perspective in life. Suicide attackers may want to hurt or kill their targets because they hold them responsible for all bad things that have happened to them or in the world, or simply just because they want to escape misery and poverty.[^cite_note-516] Based on biographies of more than seven hundred foreign fighters uncovered at an Iraqi insurgent camp, researchers believe that the motivation for suicide missions at least in Iraq was not "the global jihadi ideology", but "an explosive mix of desperation, pride, anger, sense of powerlessness, local tradition of resistance, and religious fervor".[^cite_note-What_Motivates-496]

Criminal justice professor Adam Lankford argues that suicide terrorists are not psychologically normal or stable. They are motivated to suicide and killing to mask their desire to die beneath a "veneer of heroic action" because of the religious consequences of killing themselves outright.[^cite_note-517] He has identified more than 130 individual suicide terrorists, including 9/11 ringleader [Mohamed Atta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Atta), with classic suicidal risk factors such as depression, post-traumatic stress disorder, other mental health problems, drug addictions, serious physical injuries or disabilities, having suffered the unexpected death of a loved one, or other personal crises.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELankford201361-518]

#### Nationalist resistance and religion



A 2002 [Commemorative poster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commemorative_posters_in_Palestine) of [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) suicide bomber Ashraf Sallah Alasmar in [Jenin](50997.md).

According to [Robert Pape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Pape), director of the Chicago Project on Suicide Terrorism, as of 2005, 95 percent of suicide attacks have the same specific strategic goal. This goal is to cause an occupying state to withdraw forces from a disputed territory, making nationalism their principal motivation rather than religion.[^cite_note-519]

Alternately, another source found that in Lebanon from 1983 to 1999, it was Islamists who influenced secular nationalists. Their use of suicide attacks spread to the secular groups. Five Lebanese groups "espousing a non-religious nationalist ideology" followed the lead of Islamist groups in attacking by suicide, "impressed by the effectiveness of Hezbollah's attacks in precipitating the withdrawal of the 'foreigners' from Lebanon".[^cite_note-ICT-STDC-16] In Israel suicide attacks by Islamist Islamic Jihad and Hamas also preceded those of the secular [PFLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine) and the [Al-Fatah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Fatah)-linked [Al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Martyrs'_Brigades). However, the first suicide attack in post-independence Israel was in 1972, by foreign fighters from the [Japanese Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_Red_Army) (a secular militant group) allied to [PFLP-EO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFLP-EO) unit (a secular group, led by [Wadie Haddad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadie_Haddad)).[^cite_note-F24_Ynet_KO_SA-5][^cite_note-Wadie_JC-294]

Pape found other factors associated with suicide attacks. This included the government of the targeted country being democratic and the public opinion of the country playing a role in determining policy. He also found that a difference in religion between the attackers and occupiers, and[^cite_note-520] grassroots support for the attacks[^cite_note-521] contributed. Other factors include attackers being disproportionately from the educated middle classes,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006130-522] high levels of brutality and cruelty by the occupiers,[^cite_note-523] and competition among militant groups fighting the occupiers.[^cite_note-524]

Other researchers, such as Yotam Feldner, argue that perceived religious rewards after death are instrumental in encouraging Muslims to commit suicide attacks.[^cite_note-525][^cite_note-526] These researchers contend that Pape's analysis is flawed, particularly his contention that democracies are the main targets of such attacks.[^cite_note-527] Other scholars have criticized Pape's research design, arguing that it cannot draw any conclusions on the causes of suicide terrorism.[^cite_note-Ashworth_2008-493]

Atran argues that suicide bombing has moved on from the days of Pape's study,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006130-522] where non-Islamic groups have carried out very few bombings since 2003. Instead, bombing by Muslim or Islamist groups associated with a "global ideology" of "martyrdom" has skyrocketed. In 2004 in Iraq alone, there were 400 suicide attacks and 2,000 casualties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAtran2006131-133-528] Other researchers question why prominent anti-occupation secular terrorist groups have not used suicide, such as the [Provisional IRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Irish_Republican_Army), [ETA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ETA_\(separatist_group\)), or [anti-colonialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-imperialism) insurgents in Vietnam, Algeria, and elsewhere.[^cite_note-bootSbB-529][^cite_note-FPRI-horowitz-530] They also question Pape omits that the first suicide attack in Lebanon targeted the embassy of Iraq, a country that was not occupying Lebanon.[^cite_note-bootSbB-529]

Mia Bloom agrees with Pape that competition among insurgents groups is a significant motivator, arguing the growth in suicide as a tactic is a product of "outbidding". That is, the need by competing insurgent groups to demonstrate their commitment to the cause to the broader public. This is achieved as making the ultimate sacrifice for the insurgency is a "bid" impossible to top.[^cite_note-531] This explains its use by Palestinian groups, but not that by the Tamil Tigers.[^cite_note-FPRI-horowitz-530] Still other researchers have identified sociopolitical factors as more central in the motivation of suicide attackers than religion.[^cite_note-532][^cite_note-klare-7-11-2001-533]

According to [Atran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Atran),[^cite_note-534] and former CIA case officer [Marc Sageman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Sageman),[^cite_note-535] support for suicide actions is triggered by moral outrage at perceived attacks against Islam and sacred values. However, this is converted to action as a result of small-world factors, such as being part of a football club with other *jihadis*. Millions express sympathy with global *jihad*. According to a 2006 Gallup study involving more than 50,000 interviews in dozens of countries, seven percent of the world's 1.3 billion Muslims consider the 9/11 attacks "completely justified".[^cite_note-536][^cite_note-537]

[Assaf Moghadam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assaf_Moghadam) also argues that the increase in "suicide terrorism" since 2001 is driven by [Salafi jihadist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafi_jihadism) ideology and Al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-538][^cite_note-539][^cite_note-540]

Updating his work in a 2010 book *Cutting the Fuse*, Pape reported that a close analysis of the time and location of attacks strongly support his conclusion that "foreign military occupation accounts for 98.5%—and the deployment of American combat forces for 92%—of all the 1,833 suicide terrorist attacks around the world" between 2004 and 2009.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapeFeldman201028-541] Pape wrote that, "the success attributed to the surge in 2007 and 2008 was actually less the result of an increase in coalition forces and more to a change of strategy in Baghdad and the empowerment of the Sunnis in Anbar".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapeFeldman201033-542]

The same logic can be seen in Afghanistan. In 2004 and early 2005, NATO occupied the north and west, which was controlled by the Northern Alliance, whom NATO had previously helped fight the Taliban. An enormous spike in suicide terrorism only occurred later in 2005 as NATO moved into the south and east, which had previously been controlled by the Taliban, and locals were more likely to see NATO as a foreign occupation threatening local culture and customs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapeFeldman201036-543] Critics argue the logic cannot be seen in Pakistan,[^cite_note-bootSbB-529][^cite_note-544][^cite_note-545] which has no occupation and the second highest number of suicide bombing fatalities as of mid-2015.
